# Research Target

Let \(p\) be prime and let \(d\) be an integer satisfying \(100<d<p\). The dimension is fixed at \(m=2\). Degrees \(d\le 100\), including the degenerate case \(d=0\), are outside the campaign scope and must not be treated as obstructions to the target. A point table is a function

\[
f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p.
\]

For every affine line \(L\subseteq\mathbb F_p^2\), a line table supplies a univariate polynomial \(P_L\) of degree at most \(d\), interpreted on \(L\). Define the test agreement

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)
=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)],
\]

where \(L\) is uniform among affine lines and \(x\) is uniform on \(L\).

## Current benchmark

Kominers--Thaler--Zheng prove that there are absolute constants \(C,c>0\) such that

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)\ge C(d/p)^{1/3}
\]

implies the existence of a total-degree-at-most-\(d\) polynomial \(Q\) satisfying

\[
\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P).
\]

## Target theorem

The campaign seeks progress toward the following deliberately explicit research target.

For every fixed \(\eta>0\), prove constants \(C_\eta,c_\eta>0\) and a fully stated admissible parameter regime with \(100<d<p\) such that

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)
\ge C_\eta(d/p)^{1-\eta}
\]

implies

\[
\max_{\deg Q\le d}\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]
\ge c_\eta\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P).
\]

Equivalent or stronger list-decoding conclusions are admissible only when the conversion to a single polynomial and its loss are proved.

## Milestones

- **Bivariate exponent improvement:** replace \(1/3\) by an explicit \(\alpha>1/3\) over prime fields.
- **Combinatorial improvement:** strengthen the incidence/concentration stage with its full parameter range.
- **Algebraic improvement:** strengthen the interpolation/factorization stage with complete prime-characteristic handling.
- **Near-linear exponent:** prove \((d/p)^{1-\eta}\) for every fixed \(\eta>0\).
- **Sharp threshold:** identify matching examples or prove a theorem at \(\Theta(d/p)\), if true.

Because \(d/p<1\), a larger exponent is a stronger result: it permits smaller local agreement.

The general-dimensional lift is standard once the bivariate statement is known. It is a downstream corollary, not a research milestone, and the campaign spends no agents on it.

## Lemma writing rule

**A lemma statement contains only its quantified objects, hypotheses, and conclusion. It contains no motivation, derivation, commentary, proof sketch, interpretation, history, or explanation; all such material belongs in the proof.**

The dedicated Lemma Writer post-edits every structured proof step without changing its mathematical content or status. It may split a source lemma into ordered pieces when that makes the logical structure easier to understand. Raw submissions remain immutable, and every edited statement is source-linked and checked deterministically with KaTeX before publication in the Lemma Book.

## Proof roadmap policy

Three synchronized roadmap agents maintain complete dependency DAGs for the algebraic engine, incidence engine, and end-to-end soundness chain. They share all submissions, audits, Lemma Book entries, peer roadmaps, and informal messages; each gives a majority of its attention to its assigned route. Roadmap progress is harness-derived. A node is verified only when it cites an exact source job, proof-step id, and response hash whose claim was independently accepted, and all of its dependencies are verified or external inputs. Agent work states and message-board claims never confer verification.

## Non-results

The following do not count as achieving an exponent milestone:

- a theorem only for fixed \(d\), unless clearly labeled;
- a hidden assumption \(p\ge d^C\) that makes the stated threshold vacuous;
- a list of many global polynomials without a proved single-polynomial conclusion;
- an exponent obtained after suppressing characteristic- or logarithmic losses;
- numerical success on small fields;
- a proof for line-versus-line, plane-versus-point, or axis-parallel tests presented as a line-versus-point theorem without a proved reduction.
- a result whose substantive advance concerns \(m\ne2\), extension fields, or dimension bootstrapping rather than the bivariate prime-field theorem.

IE-D-RANK-REACH [definition/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
For $d\le D<p$, let $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\},\qquad N_d(D)=\dim V_D,$$ and $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\bigoplus_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\qquad A\mapsto\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\bigr)_{M\in R}.$$ For $G=(X,T,E)$ set $$N_G(L)=\{x:(x,L)\in E\},$$ $$\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)=\{x\in N_G(L):\exists M\in R\setminus\{L\},\ (x,M)\in E\}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching if $R\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $\operatorname{rank}\Phi_{R,D}<N_d(D)$, and $|\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)|>D$ for every $L\in T$.
NOTES This replaces cardinality control of $R$ by the actual rank of its labeled graph constraints.

IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE
Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(C)<p$. If an accepted factor class $F$ has minimum degree greater than $\Delta$, satisfies $C(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ on every line vertex, and has $C_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$ at every point vertex, then there is $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$C\sim Z-Q$$ and $Q(x)=f(x)$ on every point vertex of $F$. Consequently, $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge e(F).$$
NOTES Researcher-0003 P7 is audit-inexact under a rejected compound submission; researcher-0002 P8 confirms component-level simple-root recovery but not this cross-component aggregation.

E2E-14 [definition/candidate] deps=E2E-02

researcher-0002:P7.1 Simple Roots status=proved editorial=polished
STATEMENT
Let $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ be the surviving graph from P6, and let $B$ and $\mathcal{D}$ be as in P5. Then every $x\in X_1$ satisfies

$$
B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad
B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.
$$
PROOF
The numerical hypotheses give $k_1\ge2$. Fix $x\in X_1$ and choose two distinct incident lines $L_1,L_2\in T_1$. Their direction vectors $v_1,v_2$ are linearly independent, since two distinct affine lines through one point have distinct directions in $\mathbb{F}_p^2$.

Choose parametrizations $L_i(s)=x+sv_i$. Differentiating the formal identities

$$
B(L_i(s),P_{L_i}(s))\equiv0
$$

at $s=0$ gives

$$
v_{i,X}B_X(x,f(x))+v_{i,Y}B_Y(x,f(x))
+B_Z(x,f(x))P_{L_i}'(0)=0
$$

for $i\in\{1,2\}$. If $B_Z(x,f(x))=0$, the independence of $v_1$ and $v_2$ forces

$$
B_X(x,f(x))=B_Y(x,f(x))=0.
$$

All three formal partial derivatives would then vanish at $(x,f(x))$, implying $(\mathcal{D}B)(x,f(x))=0$. This contradicts the deletion rule defining $G_1$. Hence $B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$. No derivative of a line polynomial is divided by in this argument.
DEPS P6

researcher-0002:P8.1 Polynomial Decoder status=proved editorial=polished
STATEMENT
Under the hypotheses and notation of P1–P7, there exists a polynomial $Q\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{k}{800p}.
$$
PROOF
Choose any connected component $C$ of $G_1$. Its minimum degree is at least $k_1$, and the numerical bounds give $k_1>D$. KTZ Lemmas 2.7 and 6.1 lift the line roots through one point of $C$ to a single polynomial $Q\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$. Every division in the Newton lift is by a value $B_Z(b,f(b))$, which is a nonzero field element by P7; all global polynomials produced by the lift retain total degree at most $d$.

Simple-root uniqueness propagates the same $Q$ throughout $C$, as in KTZ Lemma 6.3, without a factor-count loss. Thus $Q(x)=f(x)$ at every point vertex of $C$.

The first numerical hypothesis also implies

$$
\frac1{\sqrt{p+1}}\le\frac{k_1}{4p}.
$$

Applying the mixing argument of KTZ Lemma 6.2 to $C$ gives

$$
\frac{|X_C|}{p^2}\ge\frac{k_1}{4p}\ge\frac{k}{800p}.
$$

Since $Q=f$ on $X_C$, this is the asserted global agreement.
DEPS P7,KTZ Lemmas 2.7, 6.1, 6.2, and 6.3

  "note_markdown": "# The bivariate prime-field cubic bottleneck: support sensitivity and a modal obstruction\n\n## Abstract\n\nFor the affine line-versus-point test on `F_p²`, the KTZ cubic threshold comes from one precise chain:\n\n`|R| = O(p/ε)`, `D = Θ(sqrt(d|R|))`, and `D = O(εp)`.\n\nThe first relation is the selected-line cost, the second is weighted interpolation, and the third is simultaneously required for identity extension, deletion of nonsimple incidences, and root lifting. Their combination is `ε³ = Ω(d/p)`.\n\nRetaining the fraction σ of lines supporting an accepted carrier of edge mass δ gives the sharper proved relation `δ³ = Ω(dσ/p)`. In particular, σ=O(δ) gives a conditional exponent `1/2`, but not an unconditional benchmark improvement. In the prime regime, I also prove that the primitive squarefree interpolant satisfies `gcd(B,B_Z)=1`, so KTZ's generic directional derivative can be replaced by the vertical derivative.\n\nFinally, I give a legal obstruction at ambient degree `d=101`: modal constant line labels can attain local agreement `Θ(log p/(p log log p))` while every total-degree-at-most-101 polynomial has agreement below `2/p`. Thus a uniform exact threshold `C d/p` with global agreement `Ω(ε)` is false. This obstruction remains below every fixed-η trigger `(d/p)^(1−η)`.\n\n## Test and Notation\n\nFix a prime `p` and an integer `101≤d<p`; the dimension is exactly two. Let `L` range uniformly over the `p(p+1)` affine lines of `F_p²`, and then let `x` range uniformly over the `p` points of `L`. This is uniform sampling from the `p²(p+1)` incidences and is equivalent to first choosing `x` uniformly and then an incident line uniformly.\n\nThe point table is an arbitrary function `f:F_p²→F_p`. Each line has a univariate polynomial label `P_L` of degree at most `d`. All global degrees below are total degrees. Define\n\n`ε = Pr_{L,x∈L}[P_L(x)=f(x)]`\n\nand\n\n`Agr_d(f) = max_{Q: total-degree(Q)≤d} Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]`.\n\nAn accepted carrier is any set `E*` of accepted incidences. Write\n\n`δ = |E*|/[p²(p+1)]`,  `T = projection of E* to lines`,  `σ = |T|/[p(p+1)]`.\n\nNecessarily `0<δ≤σ≤1`. Taking all accepted incidences gives `δ=ε`; a carrier with `δ≥ε/2` retains a constant fraction of the test acceptance.\n\n## Prior Results\n\nThe primary benchmark is [Kominers–Thaler–Zheng, ECCC TR26-147, Revision 1](https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2026/147/revision/1/download/). Its Theorem 1.1, specialized to the present regime, states that absolute constants `C,c>0` give global agreement at least `cε` when `ε≥C(d/p)^(1/3)`. The theorem statement hides the constants. The bivariate proof in Sections 3–6 explicitly closes with recovery `ε/8000`.\n\nThe source chooses `k0=floor(εp/100)`, `k1=floor(εp/1000)`, a selected line set of size `O(p/ε)`, and\n\n`D = 16(floor(sqrt(d|R|))+1)`.\n\nRevision 1 uses a squarefree primitive polynomial, a generic directional derivative, and a resultant; it does not use a discriminant in the bivariate proof. The constant `10^15` below is my conservative reconstruction for a support-sensitive variant, not a constant quoted from Theorem 1.1. The older cached base report and Revision 1 must not be conflated.\n\nThe Newton step is consistent with [Harsha–Kumar–Saptharishi–Sudan, arXiv:2311.12752v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12752), especially Lemma 2.10, but the needed lifting argument is reproduced below. No Arora–Sudan theorem is used as a black box.\n\nThe active durable corpus was also audited. All eight active leaderboard files were empty, and there were no completed submissions or verifier-audit files to inherit. The [data manifest](/Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point/research_state/DATA_MANIFEST.json) is a pre-launch snapshot relative to the live campaign state, so it was treated as provenance rather than mathematical evidence. Every directory named `superseded` was excluded.\n\n## Theorem\n\n**Theorem A — support-sensitive KTZ closure.** Let `p` be prime, `101≤d<p`, and let an accepted carrier have parameters `(δ,σ)`. Put `K=10^15`. If\n\n`δ³ ≥ K dσ/p`,\n\nthen there is a polynomial `Q∈F_p[X,Y]` of total degree at most `d` with\n\n`Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)] ≥ δ/8000`.\n\nThus, if `δ≥ε/2`, the conclusion is at least `ε/16000`. With all accepted edges and σ≤1, the sufficient threshold is `ε≥10^5(d/p)^(1/3)`. Under the additional structural condition `σ≤Aδ`, it becomes the conditional square-root threshold `δ≥sqrt(10^15 A d/p)`.\n\n**Lemma B — prime vertical derivative.** If `B∈F_p[X,Y,Z]` is squarefree, primitive as a polynomial in `Z`, and has total degree below `p`, then `gcd(B,B_Z)=1`.\n\n**Theorem C — modal obstruction at the legal lower boundary.** Let `d=101`, `N=(d+1)(d+2)/2=5253`, and\n\n`r=floor(log p/(4 log log p))`.\n\nFor every sufficiently large prime, explicitly every prime `p≥max{⌈e^32⌉,100N²}`, there are a point table and constant line labels, hence labels of degree at most 101, such that\n\n`r/(130p) ≤ ε ≤ 50r/p`,  while  `Agr_101(f)<2/p`.\n\nConsequently, for every fixed `C,c>0`, arbitrarily large primes admit `ε≥C·101/p` but `Agr_101(f)<cε`.\n\n**Proposition D — near-p information baseline.** For every `101≤d<p` and every point table,\n\n`Agr_d(f) ≥ (d+1)(d+2)/(2p²)`.\n\n## Proof or Conditional Proof\n\n**[P1] (proved) Carrier pruning.** Set `k0=floor(δp/100)`. Iteratively delete point vertices and line vertices whose current carrier degree is below `k0`. Point deletions remove at most `p²k0` edges and line deletions at most `p(p+1)k0` edges. In normalized incidence measure the loss is at most\n\n`k0/(p+1)+k0/p < δ/50`.\n\nKeep the weaker bound that the remaining graph `G0` has edge mass at least `δ/4`. It has minimum degree `k0`; its line set `L0` has normalized measure λ satisfying `δ/4≤λ≤σ`.\n\n**[P2] (proved) Support-sensitive selector.** Select each line of `L0` independently with probability `θ=8/k0`. The hypothesis and `σ≥δ` imply\n\n`δ²≥10^15 d/p`,  `δp≥sqrt(10^15dp)>3·10^9`,\n\nso `k0≥δp/200`. For a fixed `L∈L0`, coverage indicators at distinct neighbors of `L` depend on disjoint sets of other lines. Each has success probability above `3/4`; Hoeffding therefore bounds failure to cover half the neighbors by `exp(−k0/8)`. Since `k0≥8 log(4p(p+1))`, a union bound over all affine lines costs less than `1/4`.\n\nIf `r=|R|`, then `E[r]=θ|L0|≥200(p+1)` and `Var(r)≤E[r]`. Chebyshev puts `r` between half and twice its mean with failure below `1/4`. Some deterministic selection consequently satisfies both coverage and\n\n`100(p+1)≤r≤16|L0|/k0≤6400σp/δ`.\n\nThese are the only Hoeffding, selector union, and Chebyshev losses.\n\n**[P3] (proved) Weighted interpolation.** Give `X,Y,Z` weights `1,1,d` and set\n\n`D=16(floor(sqrt(dr))+1)`.\n\nThen `D²>256dr` and KTZ's monomial count gives `dim(V_D)≥D³/(64d)>r(D+1)`. Each formal identity `A(L(t),P_L(t))=0` imposes at most `D+1` coefficient equations, so a nonzero interpolant exists. Moreover,\n\n`D≤1280 sqrt(dσp/δ)+16≤1280δp/sqrt(10^15)+16<δp/10000`.\n\nThus `D<p`, `D<k0/2`, and, for `k1=floor(δp/1000)`, `D<k1/2`. Also `r>D`. A nonzero `Z`-independent polynomial of degree at most `D` cannot vanish identically on more than `D` affine lines, so `A_Z≠0`. Choosing minimum weighted degree removes repeated factors and makes `A` squarefree.\n\n**[P4] (proved) Identity extension and formal content removal.** Every `L∈L0` has more than `k0/2>D` distinct accepted points that also lie on selected accepted lines. At each such point the two line labels equal the same value `f(x)`. Hence the degree-at-most-`D` polynomial `A(L(t),P_L(t))` has more than `D` distinct roots and is identically zero.\n\nWrite `A=H(X,Y)B(X,Y,Z)`, where `H` is the gcd of the coefficients in `Z`. At most `deg(H)≤D` base lines satisfy `H|_L=0` identically. On every other retained line, `H(L(t))B(L(t),P_L(t))=0` in the integral domain `F_p[t]`; hence `B(L(t),P_L(t))=0`. This is formal polynomial division, not division by values of `H`. No root multiplicities are counted.\n\n**[P5] (proved) Prime vertical-derivative lemma.** Factor the squarefree primitive polynomial as `B=c∏P_i` into distinct irreducibles over `F_p`. Primitivity implies that every `P_i` genuinely depends on `Z`. Since `deg_Z(P_i)<p`, its derivative `(P_i)_Z` is nonzero, and its smaller degree implies `P_i` does not divide `(P_i)_Z`. Modulo `P_i`,\n\n`B_Z = (P_i)_Z ∏_{j≠i}P_j ≠0`.\n\nThus no irreducible factor of `B` divides `B_Z`, proving `gcd(B,B_Z)=1`. No irreducibility over the algebraic closure is required. This replaces, in the prime-field regime only, KTZ's generic directional derivative.\n\n**[P6] (proved) Degenerate-root deletion.** Apply KTZ's resultant line bound to the coprime pair `(B,B_Z)`. Delete the at most `D` content lines, at most `D(D−1)` lines on which the two graph identities degenerate together, and at most `D` zeros of `B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))` on every other line. Their total normalized edge mass is bounded by\n\n`D/[p(p+1)] + D(D−1)/[p(p+1)] + D/p < δ/16`.\n\nPruning both sides at degree `k1=floor(δp/1000)` costs at most\n\n`k1/(p+1)+k1/p<δ/500`.\n\nThe surviving graph `G1` has edge mass at least `δ/8`, minimum degree `k1`, and `B_Z(x,f(x))≠0` at every surviving point. No discriminant or Markov inequality appears.\n\n**[P7] (proved) Hensel lifting and propagation.** At a surviving point translate the base coordinates to `(0,0)` and put `α=f(0,0)`. In `F_p[[X,Y]]`, construct a root `Φ=α+Φ_1+Φ_2+…` recursively by homogeneous degree. At stage `j`, the degree-`j` residual is canceled uniquely by division by the scalar `B_Z(0,0,α)`. Thus no factorial is inverted.\n\nEvery incident line polynomial is the same unique root after restriction to that line. There are more than `D` incident directions. Therefore every homogeneous part `Φ_j` with `d<j≤D` vanishes on more than `D≥j` projective directions and is zero. Let `Q` be the truncation through degree `d`. The polynomial `B(X,Y,Q)` has degree at most `D` and all terms through degree `D` vanish, so it is identically zero. Simple-root uniqueness propagates the same `Q` across every edge of the connected component, and `Q=f` at all of its point vertices.\n\n**[P8] (proved) Component mixing and recovery.** For a component let `a,b,e` denote its normalized point, line, and edge measures. Put `κ=k1/p` and `u=1/sqrt(p+1)`. Minimum degree and point-line mixing give\n\n`e≥κa/2`,  `e≥κb`,  and  `e≤ab+u sqrt(ab)`.\n\nThe theorem hypothesis gives `u<δ/8000≤κ/4`. If `a<κ/4`, the last two inequalities imply `9b≤a`, which contradicts the first inequality. If `b<κ/4`, they imply `a≤b`, which contradicts `e≥κb`. Hence `a,b≥κ/4≥δ/8000`. The polynomial from [P7] therefore has global agreement at least `δ/8000`. This is the sole incidence Cauchy–Schwarz loss.\n\n**[P9] (proved) Exact cubic reconstruction.** The dominant scales are\n\n`r=O(σp/δ)`,  `D=O(sqrt(dσp/δ))`,  `D=O(δp)`.\n\nThe first two give the interpolation degree; the last is independently demanded by identity extension, the `D/p` bad-incidence term, and lifting. Squaring the final comparison yields exactly `δ³=Ω(dσ/p)`. The resultant term `D²/p²` alone needs only `δ²=Ω(dσ/p)`, and mixing needs only `δ=Ω(p^−1/2)`; both are weaker here. For σ=1 this is the KTZ cubic exponent.\n\n**[P10] (conditional) Concentrated-carrier exponent.** If additionally `σ≤Aδ`, [P9] becomes `δ²=Ω(Ad/p)`, giving exponent `1/2`. Since every carrier has `σ≥δ`, this support-sensitive interpolation balance cannot exceed exponent `1/2`. This is not an unconditional improvement of the benchmark.\n\n**[P11] (proved) Modal occupancy lower bound.** For Theorem C choose all `p²` point values independently and uniformly. On a fixed line let `N_a` be the occupancy of value `a`, `M=max_a N_a`, and `Y=Σ_a 1[N_a=r]`. For one value,\n\n`Pr[N_a=r] = binom(p,r)p^(−r)(1−1/p)^(p−r) ≥ 1/(8r!)`.\n\nHere `r²≤p`, `(1−1/p)^p≥1/4`, and `r!≤r^r≤p^(1/4)`. Thus `μ=E[Y]≥p^(3/4)/8≥1`. For distinct values, the joint probability of both occupancies equaling `r` is at most `1/(r!)²`, at most 64 times the product of the lower bounds. Hence `E[Y²]≤μ+64μ²≤65μ²`. Cauchy–Schwarz, equivalently the zero-threshold Paley–Zygmund argument, gives `Pr[Y>0]≥1/65`, so `E[M]≥r/65`.\n\nGive each line a modal constant label. By linearity of expectation across lines—no line independence is asserted—the local agreement `A` satisfies `E[A]≥r/(65p)`.\n\n**[P12] (proved) Modal upper concentration.** A factorial-moment union bound gives\n\n`Pr[M≥s]≤p/s!≤p(e/s)^s`.\n\nFor `s0=ceil(4 log p/log log p)`, the tail sums to less than one and `E[M]≤s0+1≤25r`; hence `E[A]≤25r/p`. Changing one point value changes the modal count on only its `p+1` incident lines and changes `A` by at most `1/p²`. McDiarmid therefore gives\n\n`Pr[A>50r/p]≤exp(−1250r²)≤r/(520p)`.\n\nNo Markov inequality is used.\n\n**[P13] (proved) Global union bound and deterministic instance.** There are `p^N` formal total-degree-at-most-101 polynomials, with `N=5253`. For fixed `Q`, its agreement count with random `f` is `Bin(p²,1/p)`, of mean `p`. Chernoff gives\n\n`Pr[agreements(Q,f)≥2p]≤(e/4)^p`.\n\nUnion over all `p^N` polynomials gives `p^N(e/4)^p≤exp(−p/4)≤r/(520p)` under the stated lower bound on `p`. Let `G` be the event that all global agreements are below `2/p`, and `H={A≤50r/p}`. Since `0≤A≤1`,\n\n`E[A·1_{G∩H}] ≥ r/(65p)−r/(520p)−r/(520p) > r/(130p)`.\n\nSome deterministic table in `G∩H` therefore has all three bounds in Theorem C.\n\n**[P14] (refuted) Uniform exact-linear soundness.** Suppose constants `C,c>0` made `ε≥Cd/p` imply `Agr_d(f)≥cε` uniformly. Fix the legal ambient cap `d=101` and choose a sufficiently large prime with `r≥max{130·101C,260/c}`. Theorem C then has `ε≥C·101/p` but `Agr_101(f)<2/p<cε`, a contradiction. Thus the exact-linear statement is false. On the other hand, for each fixed `η>0`,\n\n`ε/(101/p)^(1−η) ≤ 50r/[101^(1−η)p^η] → 0`,\n\nso this construction does not refute a fixed-η target.\n\n**[P15] (proved) Near-p information set.** Choose distinct `a_0,…,a_d` and `b_0,…,b_d` and the triangular set `S={(a_i,b_j):i+j≤d}`. The Newton products `∏_{h<i}(X−a_h)∏_{k<j}(Y−b_k)` with `i+j≤d` form a triangular evaluation basis on `S`. Hence arbitrary prescribed values on all `|S|=(d+1)(d+2)/2` points extend to a total-degree-at-most-`d` polynomial. Applying this to `f|_S` proves Proposition D. In particular, the lower bound is `(p+1)/(2p)` at `d=p−1` and `5253/10609` at `d=101,p=103`.\n\n**[P16] (proved) Diffuse support need not concentrate.** Fix the legal ambient `d≥101`, take `f=0`, and on every line choose a degree-`d` label with `d` distinct prescribed parameter roots. Then `ε=d/p` and every line supports acceptance, so `σ=1`; nevertheless `Q=0` agrees globally everywhere. Thus local density does not imply `σ=O(ε)`, although this example is harmless for soundness.\n\n**[P17] (refuted as an obstruction) Concentrated good directions.** Let `f` be a product of `d+1` distinct homogeneous linear forms. In each of the corresponding `d+1` projective directions, every affine-line restriction loses its degree-`d+1` leading term and is a legal degree-`d` label. This gives local mass about `(d+1)/(p+1)`. But `Q=0` agrees on the union of the same `d+1` lines through the origin, a Θ(d/p) fraction. The attempted counterexample already contains the required global polynomial.\n\n**[P18] (refuted safety of omitting simplicity) Branch collision.** For odd `p` and legal ambient cap `d≥101`, `B=Z²−X²` is squarefree but its two legal branches `Q=X` and `Q=−X` meet at the origin, where `B_Z=0`. Agreement at that incidence cannot select a branch. Vertical-simple-root deletion is therefore logically necessary.\n\n**[P19] (refuted as an active obstruction) Inseparability.** A polynomial with vanishing `Z`-derivative must use positive `Z`-exponents divisible by `p`. Since the interpolant has weighted degree `D<p`, no such exponent occurs. Examples such as `Z^p−G` lie outside the interpolation space and do not obstruct the prime-field proof.\n\n## Exponent Ledger\n\n| Stage | Scale or loss | Consequence |\n|---|---|---|\n| Carrier pruning | `<δ/50`, retained as `δ/4` | Constant only |\n| Selector probability | Hoeffding `e^(−k0/8)`, union over `p(p+1)` | No δ-power loss |\n| Selector size | Chebyshev; `r≤6400σp/δ` | One factor `σ/δ` |\n| Interpolation | `dim(V_D)≈D³/d` versus `rD` constraints | `D≈sqrt(dr)` |\n| Root budget | `D≤Θ(δp)` | `δ³=Ω(dσ/p)` |\n| Content/resultant deletion | `D/p + D²/p²` | Cubic plus a weaker quadratic gate |\n| Second pruning | `<δ/500` | Constant only |\n| Mixing | One Cauchy–Schwarz error `p^−1/2` | Weaker than cubic for `d≥101` |\n| Recovery | Component point density `δ/8000` | Linear global recovery |\n| Diffuse carrier | `σ=1` | Exponent `1/3` |\n| Concentrated carrier | `σ≤Aδ` | Conditional exponent `1/2` |\n| Modal occupancy | `r≈log p/(4 log log p)` | Local `Θ(r/p)` |\n| Polynomial union | `p^5253(e/4)^p` | Global `<2/p` |\n| Exact-linear claim | Ratio at most `260/r` | Tends to zero; exponent 1 with constant threshold is false |\n\nThere is no hidden Markov loss. The only probabilistic inequalities are the selector's Hoeffding, union, and Chebyshev bounds; the modal occupancy second moment, factorial union, and McDiarmid bound; and the global Chernoff union. The sole incidence Cauchy–Schwarz loss is the `p^−1/2` mixing error.\n\n## Counterexample Attempts\n\nThe successful adversarial table is Theorem C: it is diffuse across lines and exploits the maximum occupancy among `p` symbols, not low characteristic or a small exceptional direction set. It proves that the campaign's possible sharp threshold cannot literally be a constant multiple of `d/p` with linear recovery.\n\nThe all-lines prescribed-root table [P16] shows why line-support concentration cannot be assumed. The homogeneous-direction construction [P17] fails because its zero set already supplies a global polynomial. The branch collision [P18] validates the simple-root deletion. The inseparable attempt [P19] is excluded by `D<p`. Proposition D handles `d` near `p`; it is a positive agreement baseline, not an obstruction.\n\n## Characteristic Audit\n\n- The field is exactly the prime field `F_p`; no extension-field descent is used.\n- The ambient degree always satisfies `101≤d<p`. Constant or linear objects appearing inside examples are legal labels or branches under that ambient cap; no test with parameter `d≤100` is analyzed.\n- Because `D<p`, the characteristic-pruned weighted monomial set equals the full weighted ball: every positive `Z`-exponent is strictly below `p`.\n- Lemma B rules out inseparable vertical factors after content removal. Absolute irreducibility is unnecessary.\n- KTZ's source proof uses a generic directional derivative. The replacement by `B_Z` is a new prime-field simplification and causes no exponent gain.\n- No discriminant is used. The resultant is invoked only for the proved coprime pair `(B,B_Z)`.\n- Division by `H` occurs only as an integral-domain inference on non-content lines. Hensel lifting divides only by a nonzero scalar `B_Z(x,f(x))`.\n- All interpolation constraints are coefficient identities, and every later root count uses distinct points or directions rather than multiplicity.\n- When `d` is near `p`, Theorem A's premise is vacuous because `σ≥δ` would force `p≥10^15d`; Proposition D supplies the appropriate direct check.\n\n## Limitations\n\nThe general KTZ benchmark is not improved: the exponent `1/2` conclusion requires the additional hypothesis `σ=O(δ)`, and diffuse carriers can have `σ=1`. The constant `10^15` is deliberately conservative and is not claimed optimal.\n\nThe modal obstruction refutes a uniform constant-times-`d/p` trigger but not any fixed exponent `1−η`. It instead shows that an exponent-1 formulation must include a subpolynomial occupancy allowance, at least on the order of `log p/log log p` when `d=101`.\n\nThe current selector has an upper bound `O(σp/δ)`, whereas covering more than `k0/2` points on one line requires at least `Ω(δp)` distinct witness lines. Closing this gap demands a new combinatorial or algebraic idea. No computation, higher-dimensional lifting, extension field, or individual-degree substitution is used here.",

  "note_markdown": "# Component-sensitive line concentration and the square-root conditional regime\n\n## Abstract\n\nFor the affine line-versus-point test on $\\mathbb F_p^2$, I retain the line-support measure in the Kominers--Thaler--Zheng sparsification argument. If a connected accepted-incidence subgraph has minimum degree $k$ and its line side occupies a fraction $\\tau$ of all affine lines, then a reaching family of size $O(\\tau p^2/k)$ suffices. The resulting trivariate explainer has weighted degree $D=O(\\sqrt{d\\tau p^2/k})$. Component-local accounting shows that the proof closes under\n\n$$\nk^3\\gg d\\tau p^2\\quad\\text{and}\\quad k^2\\gg dp,\n$$\n\nand yields one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial with agreement $\\Omega(k/p)$. Thus a standard pruned component with $k=\\Theta(\\epsilon p)$ and $\\tau=O(\\epsilon)$ admits the conditional square-root threshold $\\epsilon=\\Omega((d/p)^{1/2})$, with global agreement $\\Omega(\\epsilon)$.\n\nThis does not improve the unconditional $1/3$ benchmark: no argument here forces the small-component hypothesis. Moreover, a balanced-color construction using constant line polynomials proves that a single family which covers half of every accepted line can genuinely require $\\Omega(p/\\epsilon)$ lines. Hence a further improvement must be a sparsify-or-decode argument, not a uniformly smaller graph-theoretic cover.\n\n## Test and Notation\n\nFix a prime $p$ and an integer $100<d<p$; throughout $m=2$. There are $p(p+1)$ affine lines, each containing $p$ points, and every point lies on $p+1$ lines. Sampling a uniform line and then a uniform point on it is therefore the same as sampling a uniform incident pair.\n\nLet $f:\\mathbb F_p^2\\to\\mathbb F_p$, and let every affine line $L$ carry a polynomial $P_L$ of degree at most $d$. Put\n\n$$\n\\epsilon=\\Pr_{L,\\,x\\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)].\n$$\n\nThe accepted-incidence graph has an edge $(x,L)$ exactly when $x\\in L$ and $P_L(x)=f(x)$. For a subgraph $G=(X,T,E)$, write $k=\\operatorname{mindeg}(G)$, $t=|T|$, and\n\n$$\n\\tau=\\frac{t}{p(p+1)}.\n$$\n\nAll graph degrees below are accepted degrees, not ambient incidence degrees.\n\n## Prior Results\n\nThe active corpus contained no completed submission or verifier audit at the start and end of this run; every active verified, promising, rejected, and bottleneck leaderboard was `[]`. This was checked against [the durable manifest](/Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point/research_state/DATA_MANIFEST.json) and both campaign databases, excluding every directory named `superseded`.\n\nThe quoted benchmark is Theorem 1.1 of [Kominers--Thaler--Zheng, ECCC TR26-147, revision 1](https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2026/147/revision/1/download): over every finite field, $\\epsilon\\ge C(d/p)^{1/3}$ implies a single total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial with agreement at least $c\\epsilon$.\n\nThe explanation of the cubic exponent is my reconstruction from their proof, not a quoted theorem: Lemma 3.2 gives $|R|=O(p/\\epsilon)$; Lemmas 4.1--4.3 give $D=O(\\sqrt{d|R|})=O(\\sqrt{dp/\\epsilon})$; and identity extension and later lifting require $D=O(\\epsilon p)$. Squaring gives $d p/\\epsilon=O(\\epsilon^2p^2)$, or $\\epsilon^3=\\Omega(d/p)$.\n\nFor context, Theorem 4.2 of [Harsha--Kumar--Saptharishi--Sudan](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12752v1) displays the condition $p>C d/\\epsilon^7$ for its weak bivariate best-fit-oracle theorem. Calling this an exponent-$1/7$ threshold is an algebraic rewrite of that displayed condition. It is not used below.\n\n## Theorem\n\n**Theorem A (proved component-sensitive decoder).** Let $C_*=2^{40}$. Suppose $G=(X,T,E)$ is a connected subgraph of the accepted-incidence graph with minimum degree $k$ and line measure $\\tau$. If\n\n$$\nk^2\\ge C_*dp,\\qquad k^3\\ge C_*d\\tau p^2,\n$$\n\nthen there is $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that\n\n$$\n\\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge \\frac{k}{800p}.\n$$\n\nThe constant is deliberately unoptimized.\n\n**Corollary B (conditional exponent $1/2$).** Let $\\epsilon$ be the test agreement and let $G_0$ be a pruned graph supplied by KTZ Lemma 3.1, so $k_0=\\lfloor\\epsilon p/100\\rfloor$. Suppose some connected component of $G_0$ has line measure at most $\\Lambda\\epsilon$, where $\\Lambda\\ge1$. Define\n\n$$\nC_\\Lambda=\\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\\,C_*\\Lambda}.\n$$\n\nIf $\\epsilon\\ge C_\\Lambda(d/p)^{1/2}$, then some total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $Q$ satisfies\n\n$$\n\\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge\\frac{\\epsilon}{160000}.\n$$\n\nThis is conditional only in the campaign sense: the implication is proved, but the extra small-component premise is not known for every table.\n\n**Proposition C (proved global-cover obstruction).** Let $p\\ge103$ be prime and let $2\\le K\\le p/(100\\log p)$. For every campaign degree $100<d<p$, there is a valid table using constant line polynomials for which\n\n$$\n\\frac1{2K}\\le\\epsilon\\le\\frac2K,\n$$\n\nall accepted point and line degrees are within a factor two of their means, and every set $R$ which reaches at least half of the accepted points of every affine line satisfies\n\n$$\n|R|\\ge\\frac{Kp}{16}\\ge\\frac{p}{32\\epsilon}.\n$$\n\nNevertheless, one constant global polynomial agrees with $f$ on at least $\\epsilon/2$ of the points.\n\n## Proof or Conditional Proof\n\n**[P1] (proved: component size).** Apply KTZ Lemma 2.1 to the point and line sides of $G$. Its exact mixing error is $\\sqrt{\\mu(X)\\mu(T)}/\\sqrt{p+1}$. The first hypothesis and $d>100$ imply $1/\\sqrt{p+1}\\le k/(4p)$. The argument of KTZ Lemma 6.2 therefore gives\n\n$$\n\\mu(X),\\tau\\ge\\frac{k}{4p}.\n$$\n\nIn particular, $t\\ge k(p+1)/4$.\n\n**[P2] (proved: component-sensitive reaching family).** Select each line of $T$ independently with probability $8/k$. For $x\\in N_G(L)$, at least $k-1$ other accepted lines pass through $x$, so $x$ is reached with probability at least $3/4$. For distinct points of a fixed affine line $L$, the relevant sets of other lines are disjoint: two such points have only $L$ as a common line. The reach indicators are consequently independent. Chernoff gives failure probability at most $e^{-\\deg_G(L)/24}\\le e^{-k/24}$ for a fixed $L$. The hypotheses imply $k\\gg\\log p$, so a union bound over fewer than $2p^2$ lines succeeds. A second Chernoff bound controls the sample size. Hence one choice $R$ has, with $r=|R|$,\n\n$$\n\\frac{4t}{k}\\le r\\le\\frac{16t}{k},\n$$\n\nand reaches more than half of $N_G(L)$ for every $L\\in T$. By [P1], $r\\ge p+1$.\n\n**[P3] (proved: interpolation).** Set\n\n$$\nD=16\\bigl(\\lfloor\\sqrt{dr}\\rfloor+1\\bigr).\n$$\n\nThen $D^2>256dr$ and\n\n$$\nD^2\\le1024dr\\le\\frac{16384dt}{k}.\n$$\n\nUsing $t=\\tau p(p+1)\\le2\\tau p^2$ and $k^3\\ge C_*d\\tau p^2$ gives $D/k<1/5000$. Thus $D<k\\le p$, while $r\u0003e p>D$.\n\nKTZ Lemma 2.2 supplies at least $D^3/(64d)$ eligible monomials of weighted degree at most $D$. Each identity $A(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$ costs at most $D+1$ homogeneous conditions, and $D^2>256dr$ makes the number of variables exceed $r(D+1)$. A nonzero interpolant exists. Because $r>D$, it cannot be independent of $Z$; the allowed $Z$-exponents make $A_Z\\ne0$. Choose one of minimum weighted degree. KTZ Lemma 2.3 makes it squarefree.\n\n**[P4] (proved: extension).** If $x$ is an accepted point of $L$ reached by $M\\in R\\setminus\\{L\\}$, then $P_L(x)=f(x)=P_M(x)$ and hence $A(x,P_L(x))=0$. More than $k/2>D$ distinct such points lie on every $L\\in T$. Since $A(L(t),P_L(t))$ has degree at most $D$, it vanishes identically. No Markov loss occurs here.\n\n**[P5] (proved: characteristic-safe cleanup).** Write $A=HB$, where $H(X,Y)$ is the gcd of the coefficients of $A$ as a polynomial in $Z$. At most $D$ lines make $H|_L$ identically zero. On every other line, exact division in the domain $\\mathbb F_p[t]$ gives $B(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$. The squarefree $B$ has degree below $p$. KTZ Lemma 2.4 supplies a formal directional derivative $\\mathcal D$ with $\\gcd(B,\\mathcal DB)=1$. KTZ Lemma 2.6, via the resultant, bounds lines on whose polynomial graph both vanish by $D(D-1)$. Every other line has at most $D$ accepted points where $(\\mathcal DB)(x,f(x))=0$. Thus at most\n\n$$\npD^2+Dt\n$$\n\naccepted edges are deleted.\n\n**[P6] (proved: local deletion and pruning).** Since $|E|\\ge kt$,\n\n$$\n\\frac{pD^2+Dt}{|E|}\\le\\frac{D}{k}+\\frac{16384dp}{k^2}<\\frac1{4000}.\n$$\n\nPut $k_1=\\lfloor k/100\\rfloor$. Iteratively deleting vertices of current degree below $k_1$ removes fewer than\n\n$$\nk_1(|X|+t)\\le 2(k_1/k)|E|\\le |E|/50\n$$\n\nadditional edges. A nonempty graph of minimum degree $k_1$ remains, although it may fragment.\n\n**[P7] (proved: simple roots).** At every remaining point choose two incident lines; $k_1\\ge2$. Their directions are independent in $\\mathbb F_p^2$. Differentiating their formal identities, if $B_Z(x,f(x))=0$, forces $B_X(x,f(x))=B_Y(x,f(x))=0$. This contradicts the retained condition $(\\mathcal DB)(x,f(x))\\ne0$. Hence every retained value is a simple $Z$-root. No derivative is divided by in this step.\n\n**[P8] (proved: lift, propagate, and measure).** Choose any connected component of the graph left by [P6]. Its minimum degree is $k_1>D$. KTZ Lemmas 2.7 and 6.1 lift the line roots through one point to a single total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $Q$. Simple-root uniqueness propagates the same $Q$ through the component, as in KTZ Lemma 6.3; there is no factor-count loss. The first numerical hypothesis also gives $1/\\sqrt{p+1}\\le k_1/(4p)$. KTZ Lemma 6.2 then gives point measure at least\n\n$$\n\\frac{k_1}{4p}\\ge\\frac{k}{800p},\n$$\n\nproving Theorem A.\n\n**[P9] (conditional: translate to local agreement).** In Corollary B, the stated threshold makes $k_0\\ge\\epsilon p/200$. The inequality $\\epsilon^2p\\ge8{,}000{,}000C_*\\Lambda d$ implies both\n\n$$\nk_0^2\\ge C_*dp,\n\\qquad\nk_0^3\\ge C_*d(\\Lambda\\epsilon)p^2.\n$$\n\nTheorem A therefore gives agreement at least $k_0/(800p)\\ge\\epsilon/160000$. This is the exponent-$1/2$ calculation. Its only unresolved campaign premise is the existence of the component with $\\tau\\le\\Lambda\\epsilon$.\n\n**[P10] (proved: realizable balanced-color graph).** Independently color every point and every affine line uniformly from $[K]$, inject the colors into $\\mathbb F_p$, put $f(x)$ equal to the point color, and make $P_L$ the corresponding constant line color. Conditional on a vertex color, its accepted degree is binomial with mean $p/K$ on the line side or $(p+1)/K$ on the point side. A Chernoff bound followed by a union bound over fewer than $3p^2$ vertices has failure probability at most $6p^2e^{-p/(12K)}<1$. Therefore a deterministic realization with the claimed degree bounds exists. These constant line polynomials are degree at most the fixed campaign degree $d>100$; this is not an analysis of the excluded case $d=0$.\n\n**[P11] (proved: cover lower bound).** Let $S$ be the points incident to an accepted edge from $R$. The line-degree upper bound gives $|S|\\le2p|R|/K$. If every line has at least half its accepted points in $S$, summing over all $p(p+1)$ lines gives at least $p^2(p+1)/(4K)$ accepted incidences from $S$. The point-degree upper bound is $2(p+1)/K$, so $|S|\\ge p^2/8$. Hence $|R|\\ge Kp/16$. Since $\\epsilon\\ge1/(2K)$, this is at least $p/(32\\epsilon)$. Finally, $f$ takes only $K$ values, so one constant has agreement at least $1/K\\ge\\epsilon/2$.\n\n## Exponent Ledger\n\n| Stage | Input | Output/loss |\n|---|---|---|\n| KTZ reconstruction | $|R|=O(p/\\epsilon)$ | $D=O(\\sqrt{dp/\\epsilon})$; $D<\\epsilon p$ gives $\\epsilon^3\\gtrsim d/p$ |\n| Component sampling | $t=\\tau p(p+1)$, degree $k$ | $|R|=\\Theta(t/k)=\\Theta(\\tau p^2/k)$; Chernoff/union losses are constant |\n| Interpolation | $r=|R|$ | $D=\\Theta(\\sqrt{dr})=O(\\sqrt{d\\tau p^2/k})$ |\n| Extension | root supply $k$ | $D<k$ requires $k^3\\gtrsim d\\tau p^2$ |\n| Degenerate deletion | bad edges $pD^2+Dt$ | relative loss $O(dp/k^2+D/k)$, requiring $k^2\\gtrsim dp$ |\n| Mixing | minimum degree $k_1$ | $p^{-1/2}\\lesssim k_1/p$; absorbed because $d>100$ and $k^2\\gg dp$ |\n| Global agreement | surviving component | $\\Pr[Q=f]\\ge k/(800p)$; no power loss |\n| Small-component corollary | $k=\\Theta(\\epsilon p)$, $\\tau=O(\\epsilon)$ | both constraints become $\\epsilon^2\\gtrsim d/p$ |\n| Global-cover obstruction | $\\epsilon=\\Theta(1/K)$ | every uniform cover has $|R|=\\Omega(Kp)=\\Omega(p/\\epsilon)$ |\n\n## Counterexample Attempts\n\nThe balanced-color table proves that the $p/\\epsilon$ global-cover scale is not merely an artifact of independent sampling. It is an actual degree-at-most-$d$ line-versus-point table. It does not refute soundness, because a constant decoder already has agreement $\\Omega(\\epsilon)$.\n\nConcentrating all good lines in a subset of directions also does not refute the conditional result. With at least two good directions, the accepted graph is connected, has line measure $\\tau=\\epsilon$, and is governed by the planted polynomial.\n\nIndependent random tables accept with probability about $1/p$, far below the campaign range. Attempts using $t^p-t$ or other inseparable functional aliases require line degree at least $p$ and are excluded by $d<p$.\n\nAt the lower boundary $d=101$, the square-root scale decreases from about $0.9902$ at $p=103$ to $0.1005$ at $p=10007$. When $d$ approaches $p$, both cube-root and square-root scales approach one, and the large-constant hypotheses become vacuous. No near-$p$ counterexample is claimed.\n\n## Characteristic Audit\n\nThe proof is entirely over the prime field and keeps $D<p$. Formal and functional identities cannot be confused for line polynomials because $d<p$. The eligible-monomial restriction ensures $A_Z\\ne0$. Squarefreeness and perfectness rule out an inseparable irreducible factor with all partial derivatives zero. The generic derivative is chosen over $\\mathbb F_p$ using fewer than $p$ exceptional hyperplanes. Resultants, rather than discriminant division, control common line roots. Newton lifting divides only by a certified nonzero simple-root derivative. Ordinary interpolation is used with no hidden multiplicities or Hasse-derivative assumptions.\n\n## Limitations\n\nThe result does not prove that every accepted core contains a component with $\\tau=O(\\epsilon)$. A single connected component may have $\\tau$ close to one, in which case the new parameter law returns the cubic benchmark. The explicit constant $2^{40}$ is safe but intentionally very loose, making the finite nonvacuous range much smaller than the exponent notation suggests. Proposition C obstructs only a single cover required to work for every line; it does not obstruct an algebraically informed decoder or an adaptive localization argument. Finite computations above are falsification checks only and play no role in the asymptotic proof.",

=== Uniform Normal Multiplicity Is Exponent-Neutral in the Bivariate KTZ Interpolation Step ===
STATUS proved
THEOREM For prime p, 100<d<p, distinct selected line graphs R of size n, multiplicity s>=1, and d<=D<p, order-s graph-ideal vanishing imposes at most n C_s(D,d) equations, where C_s(D,d)=sum_{a+b<s} max(D-a-db+1,0). If N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d), with N_d(D)=sum_{j<=D/d} binom(D-dj+2,2), a nonzero interpolant exists. Each compatible transverse intersection supplies an s-fold root, so h such points transfer the identity when sh>D. The explicit choice D_0=floor(4sqrt(dns(s+1)))+1 suffices when d<=D_0<p and D_0<sh. Conversely, in the KTZ regime n>D, any proof using only this raw dimension criterion and root count must satisfy h^2>dn/12. Thus at n=Theta(p/epsilon), h=Theta(epsilon p), uniform multiplicity retains the cubic condition epsilon^3=Omega(d/p).
CONCLUSION A fully quantified transverse-jet interpolation lemma and a rigorous architecture-specific obstruction were proved. Uniform higher multiplicity cannot improve the cubic exponent when existence is certified by raw coefficient-versus-jet counting and propagation is certified by univariate root multiplicity. The result neither improves nor refutes the bivariate soundness benchmark. The only final global-agreement guarantee remains the quoted KTZ conclusion, at least epsilon/8000 in its m=2 proof.

THEOREM For prime p, 100<d<p, distinct selected line graphs R of size n, multiplicity s>=1, and d<=D<p, order-s graph-ideal vanishing imposes at most n C_s(D,d) equations, where C_s(D,d)=sum_{a+b<s} max(D-a-db+1,0). Each compatible transverse intersection supplies an s-fold root, so h such points transfer the identity when sh>D. The explicit choice D_0=floor(4sqrt(dns(s+1)))+1 suffices when d<=D_0<p and D_0<sh. Conversely, in the KTZ regime n>D, any proof using only this raw dimension criterion and root count must satisfy h^2>dn/12. Thus at n=Theta(p/epsilon), h=Theta(epsilon p), uniform multiplicity retains the cubic condition epsilon^3=Omega(d/p).
CONCLUSION A fully quantified transverse-jet interpolation lemma and a rigorous architecture-specific obstruction were proved. Uniform higher multiplicity cannot improve the cubic exponent when existence is certified by raw coefficient-versus-jet counting and propagation is certified by univariate root multiplicity. The result neither improves nor refutes the bivariate soundness benchmark. The only final global-agreement guarantee remains the quoted KTZ conclusion, at least epsilon/8000 in its m=2 proof.
OBSTRUCTIONS
["Uniform graph multiplicity has quadratic normal-jet cost but only linear root-multiplicity benefit.","The rank-blind coefficient-versus-equation architecture forces h^2>dn/12, independent of multiplicity.","Order at least two makes all first derivatives vanish along selected graphs, conflicting with simple-root Newton-Hensel lifting.","Squarefree reduction and content division preserve ordinary zero sets but not higher-order graph vanishing.","Under D<p, primitive multiplicity s requires D>=sd; hence no s>=2 is available when d>p/2.","Ordinary value consistency supplies no derivative or jet compatibility across an accepted intersection."]
STEPS
P4 [proved] deps=P1,P3
S: Nontrivial Z-dependence and the primitive degree cost are fully quantified.
P: A Z-independent solution is divisible by the s-th power of every distinct line equation, so D>=sn. More generally, if deg_Z A=z<s, then each line equation occurs to power s-z; n>D forces z>=s and D>=sd. With D<p, any Z-dependent A has A_Z nonzero.

=== Hasse-Height Stratification and Factorwise Simple-Root Cleanup ===
STATUS proved
THEOREM For every prime p and integer 101<=d<p, a squarefree Z-dependent interpolant A of weighted degree a, with weights (1,1,d), admits an explicit factorwise cleanup. If A=HB, B=c product_i C_i, Delta=wdeg(B), and iota is the weighted degree of gcd(B,B_Z), then at most h+floor((Delta^2-dDelta+(d-1)iota)/d) content or exceptional graph lines are removed. Every remaining separable-type line loses at most min(p,Delta-d) points, every inseparable-type line at most min(p,Delta-1), and every retained point of graph degree at least two satisfies B_Z nonzero. Each irreducible factor has a p-power Hasse height s with weighted degree at least ds, and its height-s stratum obeys the common-graph bound (2r_s-s)Delta_s-d r_s^2. In particular, Delta<pd implies gcd(B,B_Z)=1; if a<=D<pd, at most floor(D^2/d-D) lines and D-d points per remaining line are lost. The threshold pd is sharp: Z^p-XY^(p-1) refutes any lifting rule based solely on multiplicity-weighted direction count. Under the quoted KTZ premise epsilon>=C_KTZ(d/p)^(1/3), the final global agreement remains epsilon/8000.
CONCLUSION The factorwise cleanup, Hasse-height stratification, and sharp weighted-degree separability threshold are proved. They close a characteristic-p hypothesis gap and refute a tempting multiplicity-weighted lifting shortcut, but they do not improve the bivariate benchmark. Under the quoted KTZ hypothesis epsilon >= C_KTZ(d/p)^(1/3), the final global agreement remains at least epsilon/8000. The (d/p)^(1-o(1)) target remains open.
OBSTRUCTIONS
["A multiplicity-weighted count of graph directions is insufficient: Z^p-XY^(p-1) exceeds the weighted-degree count yet has no formal section.","In the actual KTZ parameter regime D<p, inseparability is automatically absent because the sharp danger threshold is pd. Hasse derivatives therefore repair hypotheses but do not attack the active bottleneck.","The per-line root loss D/p and the identity-extension requirement D=O(epsilon p) both reconstruct the cubic inequality epsilon^3=Omega(d/p).","The selector estimate n<=6400p/epsilon is one-sided. Treating it as n=Theta(p/epsilon) would turn a sufficient worst-case envelope into an unsupported necessity claim.","Factorwise cleanup does not itself consolidate surviving incidences onto one irreducible component; that global component-selection problem remains.","Formal graph identities cannot be inferred from agreement at all p parameter values when the substituted degree may reach p; t^p-t aliasing must remain excluded."]
STEPS
[P1] [proved] deps=
S: Every irreducible Z-dependent factor has a well-defined p-power Hasse height s; lower positive Hasse derivatives vanish, the order-s derivative is nonzero and coprime, and its weighted degree is at least ds.
P: Write C=sum_k c_k Z^k. All positive supported k are divisible by s, and one has k/s nonzero modulo p. Lucas's theorem gives binom(k,r)=0 for 0<r<s and a nonzero coefficient at r=s. The order-s derivative has smaller Z-degree, hence is coprime to irreducible C. Z-dependence gives weighted degree at least ds.

STEPS
[P6] [proved] deps=[P3],[P5]
S: The factorwise exceptional-line count is at most sum_i floor(delta_i(delta_i-omega_i)/d)+sum_{i<j} floor(delta_i delta_j/d), hence at most floor((Delta^2-dDelta+(d-1)iota)/d).
P: Apply [P3] to every factor pair and to each factor with its selected derivative. An inseparable irreducible factor has a nonzero X- or Y-derivative because F_p is perfect. Summing and using sum omega_i delta_i=d(Delta-iota)+iota yields the claimed bound.

=== Hasse-Height Stratification and Factorwise Simple-Root Cleanup ===
STATUS proved
THEOREM For every prime p and integer 101<=d<p, a squarefree Z-dependent interpolant A of weighted degree a, with weights (1,1,d), admits an explicit factorwise cleanup. If A=HB, B=c product_i C_i, Delta=wdeg(B), and iota is the weighted degree of gcd(B,B_Z), then at most h+floor((Delta^2-dDelta+(d-1)iota)/d) content or exceptional graph lines are removed. Every remaining separable-type line loses at most min(p,Delta-d) points, every inseparable-type line at most min(p,Delta-1), and every retained point of graph degree at least two satisfies B_Z nonzero. Each irreducible factor has a p-power Hasse height s with weighted degree at least ds, and its height-s stratum obeys the common-graph bound (2r_s-s)Delta_s-d r_s^2. In particular, Delta<pd implies gcd(B,B_Z)=1; if a<=D<pd, at most floor(D^2/d-D) lines and D-d points per remaining line are lost. The threshold pd is sharp: Z^p-XY^(p-1) refutes any lifting rule based solely on multiplicity-weighted direction count. Under the quoted KTZ premise epsilon>=C_KTZ(d/p)^(1/3), the final global agreement remains epsilon/8000.
CONCLUSION The factorwise cleanup, Hasse-height stratification, and sharp weighted-degree separability threshold are proved. They close a characteristic-p hypothesis gap and refute a tempting multiplicity-weighted lifting shortcut, but they do not improve the bivariate benchmark. Under the quoted KTZ hypothesis epsilon >= C_KTZ(d/p)^(1/3), the final global agreement remains at least epsilon/8000. The (d/p)^(1-o(1)) target remains open.

["A multiplicity-weighted count of graph directions is insufficient: Z^p-XY^(p-1) exceeds the weighted-degree count yet has no formal section.","In the actual KTZ parameter regime D<p, inseparability is automatically absent because the sharp danger threshold is pd. Hasse derivatives therefore repair hypotheses but do not attack the active bottleneck.","The per-line root loss D/p and the identity-extension requirement D=O(epsilon p) both reconstruct the cubic inequality epsilon^3=Omega(d/p).","The selector estimate n<=6400p/epsilon is one-sided. Treating it as n=Theta(p/epsilon) would turn a sufficient worst-case envelope into an unsupported necessity claim.","Factorwise cleanup does not itself consolidate surviving incidences onto one irreducible component; that global component-selection problem remains.","Formal graph identities cannot be inferred from agreement at all p parameter values when the substituted degree may reach p; t^p-t aliasing must remain excluded."]
STEPS
[P7] [proved] deps=[P6]
S: After the exact per-line root deletion, the normalized loss is the theorem's content, exceptional-line, separable-root, and inseparable-root expression, with optional extra loss 1/(p+1).
P: On a unique-factor line, the selected derivative and every other factor restrict to nonzero univariate polynomials. Their degree sum is Delta-omega_i, capped at p. Normalizing line and point deletions in the p^2(p+1)-incidence space gives the stated loss. Degree-at-most-one point pruning costs at most p^2 edges.

=== Frobenius Purification and the Singular-Lift Bottleneck ===
STATUS proved
THEOREM For prime p and 101≤d<p: (i) an F_p-irreducible Z-dependent factor of weighted degree δ and Z-degree r with k≥2 absolute factors supports at most (2rδ-dr²)/k²≤δ²/(4d) degree-d graph lines; (ii) for a fixed factor, smooth ramified accepted lifts contribute normalized incidence loss at most 1/(p+1); (iii) if d≤D<2d, a primitive Z-dependent weight-D polynomial is absolutely irreducible and linear in Z, with at most D(D-d) ramified base points; and (iv) for every d≤p-2 there is a minimum-weight D=d+1 primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible interpolant B=XZ+H(Y), whose raw resultant X=0 is a smooth absolutely irreducible plane curve, supporting sp>D fully accepted lines with one ramified point each but no total-degree-d polynomial matching all selected labels. These assertions isolate singular lifted points as the remaining obstruction and do not improve the global soundness exponent.
CONCLUSION A proved architecture-level correction was obtained, but not a stronger soundness exponent. Geometrically reducible factors can be purged by a sharp Frobenius–resultant bound, and smooth ramified lifts cost at most 1/(p+1) for a fixed factor. The unresolved obstruction is singular accepted lifts: an explicit minimum-weight absolutely irreducible interpolant has a smooth absolutely irreducible resultant curve, supports more than D fully accepted graph lines, and still has no global polynomial root matching all labels. Thus absolute irreducibility and plane-curve point counts alone do not improve the KTZ exponent 1/3.
OBSTRUCTIONS
["Singular accepted lifts may support p distinct graph directions even when the auxiliary polynomial is primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible, vertically separable, and minimum-weight.","A raw resultant can define a smooth absolutely irreducible plane curve while its lifted fibers contain singular points of the auxiliary hypersurface; plane-curve point counts do not see this directional multiplicity.","The standard normalized discriminant may divide away a nonconstant leading coefficient and entirely miss degree-dropping fibers.","The sharp purification loss D²/[4d p(p+1)] becomes O(1/(εp)) at the KTZ interpolation envelope, introducing an ε²p condition if all geometrically reducible factors are discarded.","The examples obstruct simple-root and deletion architectures but do not upper-bound agreement with every global degree-d polynomial, so they are not counterexamples to KTZ soundness or the fixed-η target."]
STEPS
P1 [proved] deps=
S: Squarefree, Z-primitive B with weighted degree below dp satisfies gcd(B,B_Z)=1.
P: If an irreducible C divides squarefree B and B_Z, then C_Z=0. Since deg_Z(C)<p, C is Z-independent, contradicting primitivity. Z^p-X proves sharpness at weighted degree dp.

=== Frobenius Purification and the Singular-Lift Bottleneck ===
STATUS proved
THEOREM For prime p and 101≤d<p: (i) an F_p-irreducible Z-dependent factor of weighted degree δ and Z-degree r with k≥2 absolute factors supports at most (2rδ-dr²)/k²≤δ²/(4d) degree-d graph lines; (ii) for a fixed factor, smooth ramified accepted lifts contribute normalized incidence loss at most 1/(p+1); (iii) if d≤D<2d, a primitive Z-dependent weight-D polynomial is absolutely irreducible and linear in Z, with at most D(D-d) ramified base points; and (iv) for every d≤p-2 there is a minimum-weight D=d+1 primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible interpolant B=XZ+H(Y), whose raw resultant X=0 is a smooth absolutely irreducible plane curve, supporting sp>D fully accepted lines with one ramified point each but no total-degree-d polynomial matching all selected labels. These assertions isolate singular lifted points as the remaining obstruction and do not improve the global soundness exponent.
CONCLUSION A proved architecture-level correction was obtained, but not a stronger soundness exponent. Geometrically reducible factors can be purged by a sharp Frobenius–resultant bound, and smooth ramified lifts cost at most 1/(p+1) for a fixed factor. The unresolved obstruction is singular accepted lifts: an explicit minimum-weight absolutely irreducible interpolant has a smooth absolutely irreducible resultant curve, supports more than D fully accepted graph lines, and still has no global polynomial root matching all labels. Thus absolute irreducibility and plane-curve point counts alone do not improve the KTZ exponent 1/3.
OBSTRUCTIONS
["Singular accepted lifts may support p distinct graph directions even when the auxiliary polynomial is primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible, vertically separable, and minimum-weight.","A raw resultant can define a smooth absolutely irreducible plane curve while its lifted fibers contain singular points of the auxiliary hypersurface; plane-curve point counts do not see this directional multiplicity.","The standard normalized discriminant may divide away a nonconstant leading coefficient and entirely miss degree-dropping fibers.","The sharp purification loss D²/[4d p(p+1)] becomes O(1/(εp)) at the KTZ interpolation envelope, introducing an ε²p condition if all geometrically reducible factors are discarded.","The examples obstruct simple-root and deletion architectures but do not upper-bound agreement with every global degree-d polynomial, so they are not counterexamples to KTZ soundness or the fixed-η target."]

S: All geometrically reducible factors of a primitive weight-D interpolant support at most D²/(4d) graph lines in total, and the constant is sharp.
P: Assign each graph line to an F_p-irreducible factor and use Σδ_i²≤D². The example Z²-νH_0² attains δ²/(4d)=d lines.

TITLE Hasse-Height Stratification and Factorwise Simple-Root Cleanup
THEOREM
For every prime p and integer 101<=d<p, a squarefree Z-dependent interpolant A of weighted degree a, with weights (1,1,d), admits an explicit factorwise cleanup. If A=HB, B=c product_i C_i, Delta=wdeg(B), and iota is the weighted degree of gcd(B,B_Z), then at most h+floor((Delta^2-dDelta+(d-1)iota)/d) content or exceptional graph lines are removed. Every remaining separable-type line loses at most min(p,Delta-d) points, every inseparable-type line at most min(p,Delta-1), and every retained point of graph degree at least two satisfies B_Z nonzero. Each irreducible factor has a p-power Hasse height s with weighted degree at least ds, and its height-s stratum obeys the common-graph bound (2r_s-s)Delta_s-d r_s^2. In particular, Delta<pd implies gcd(B,B_Z)=1; if a<=D<pd, at most floor(D^2/d-D) lines and D-d points per remaining line are lost. The threshold pd is sharp: Z^p-XY^(p-1) refutes any lifting rule based solely on multiplicity-weighted direction count. Under the quoted KTZ premise epsilon>=C_KTZ(d/p)^(1/3), the final global agreement remains epsilon/8000.
CONCLUSION
The factorwise cleanup, Hasse-height stratification, and sharp weighted-degree separability threshold are proved. They close a characteristic-p hypothesis gap and refute a tempting multiplicity-weighted lifting shortcut, but they do not improve the bivariate benchmark. Under the quoted KTZ hypothesis epsilon >= C_KTZ(d/p)^(1/3), the final global agreement remains at least epsilon/8000. The (d/p)^(1-o(1)) target remains open.
CHAR
Hasse derivatives are defined coefficientwise by F(Z+T)=sum_r partial_Z^[r]F T^r; no factorials or division by r! occur.
- Lucas's theorem is used to prove vanishing of lower Hasse derivatives for p-power Z-exponents.
- The perfectness of F_p is used only to show that an irreducible polynomial whose three ordinary partial derivatives vanish would be a nontrivial p-th power.
- For squarefree B, gcd(B,B_Z) is exactly the product of irreducible factors with zero ordinary Z-derivative.
- A Z-dependent factor with zero Z-derivative has weighted degree at least pd. Thus weighted degree below pd, not merely below p, is the sharp automatic-separability range.
- Formal polynomial identities in F_p[t] are kept distinct from agreement on the p field elements; no reduction modulo t^p-t is used.
- Content is removed using the integral-domain implication H(L)B(L,q)=0 and H(L) nonzero implies B(L,q)=0. No pointwise division by H occurs.
- The resultant proof uses a Sylvester determinant and line factors. It does not divide by leading coefficients or invoke a discriminant.
- Only irreducibility over F_p[X,Y,Z] is needed; absolute irreducibility is not assumed.
- The cleanup never divides by a derivative. A downstream Newton step may divide only after the relevant scalar value B_Z(x,y,z) has been certified nonzero.
- All root-union and factor-pair bounds are deterministic sums. There is no Markov or Cauchy–Schwarz loss in the new lemma.
- The KTZ selector's concentration and union estimates remain inside quoted Lemma 3.2; its affine mixing term is of order p^(-1/2).
- No multiplicity interpolation is introduced. The polynomial Z^p-XY^(p-1) shows why vertical multiplicity cannot simply replace distinct graph constraints.
- The lower campaign boundary p=103,d=101 and the near-p case d=p-1 were checked explicitly.
OBSTRUCTIONS
A multiplicity-weighted count of graph directions is insufficient: Z^p-XY^(p-1) exceeds the weighted-degree count yet has no formal section.
- In the actual KTZ parameter regime D<p, inseparability is automatically absent because the sharp danger threshold is pd. Hasse derivatives therefore repair hypotheses but do not attack the active bottleneck.
- The per-line root loss D/p and the identity-extension requirement D=O(epsilon p) both reconstruct the cubic inequality epsilon^3=Omega(d/p).
- The selector estimate n<=6400p/epsilon is one-sided. Treating it as n=Theta(p/epsilon) would turn a sufficient worst-case envelope into an unsupported necessity claim.
- Factorwise cleanup does not itself consolidate surviving incidences onto one irreducible component; that global component-selection problem remains.
- Formal graph identities cannot be inferred from agreement at all p parameter values when the substituted degree may reach p; t^p-t aliasing must remain excluded.
STEPS
[P1] [proved] deps=
S: Every irreducible Z-dependent factor has a well-defined p-power Hasse height s; lower positive Hasse derivatives vanish, the order-s derivative is nonzero and coprime, and its weighted degree is at least ds.
P: Write C=sum_k c_k Z^k. All positive supported k are divisible by s, and one has k/s nonzero modulo p. Lucas's theorem gives binom(k,r)=0 for 0<r<s and a nonzero coefficient at r=s. The order-s derivative has smaller Z-degree, hence is coprime to irreducible C. Z-dependence gives weighted degree at least ds.
[P2] [proved] deps=[P1]
S: A height-s graph root has vertical multiplicity at least s, and gcd(B,B_Z) is exactly the product of height-greater-than-one factors.
P: Writing C=E(Z^s), a graph root q gives divisibility by Z^s-q^s=(Z-q)^s. Reducing the ordinary product rule for squarefree B modulo each factor shows that the factor divides B_Z exactly when its ordinary Z-derivative vanishes.
[P3] [proved] deps=
S: Coprime weighted-degree u,v polynomials of Z-degrees r,s have at most su+rv-drs <= uv/d common affine polynomial graph lines.
P: The nonzero Sylvester resultant has XY-degree at most su+rv-drs. Every common graph line supplies a distinct affine linear factor. The inequality with uv/d follows from (u-dr)(v-ds)>=0. Zero Z-degree cases reduce directly to base-polynomial line factors.
[P4] [proved] deps=[P1],[P3]
S: A height-s factor stratum has at most (2r_s-s)Delta_s-d r_s^2 graph lines common with its first nonzero Hasse derivative.
P: The Hasse product rule leaves only one order-s derivative at a time, so the stratum product is coprime to its order-s derivative. That derivative has weighted degree at most Delta_s-ds and Z-degree at most r_s-s. Substitution in [P3] gives the displayed formula.
[P5] [proved] deps=
S: Removing at most h content lines reduces every formal A-graph identity to a formal identity for at least one primitive factor of B.
P: From H(L)B(L,q)=0 in the integral domain F_p[t], either H vanishes identically or B(L,q)=0. At most h lines divide H. The factor product identity then forces at least one irreducible factor identity.
[P6] [proved] deps=[P3],[P5]
S: The factorwise exceptional-line count is at most sum_i floor(delta_i(delta_i-omega_i)/d)+sum_{i<j} floor(delta_i delta_j/d), hence at most floor((Delta^2-dDelta+(d-1)iota)/d).
P: Apply [P3] to every factor pair and to each factor with its selected derivative. An inseparable irreducible factor has a nonzero X- or Y-derivative because F_p is perfect. Summing and using sum omega_i delta_i=d(Delta-iota)+iota yields the claimed bound.
[P7] [proved] deps=[P6]
S: After the exact per-line root deletion, the normalized loss is the theorem's content, exceptional-line, separable-root, and inseparable-root expression, with optional extra loss 1/(p+1).
P: On a unique-factor line, the selected derivative and every other factor restrict to nonzero univariate polynomials. Their degree sum is Delta-omega_i, capped at p. Normalizing line and point deletions in the p^2(p+1)-incidence space gives the stated loss. Degree-at-most-one point pruning costs at most p^2 edges.
[P8] [proved] deps=[P7]
S: Every retained point incident to at least two retained graph lines is a simple vertical point of B.
P: At an intersection, uniqueness forces both graph lines onto the same factor. If that factor were inseparable, differentiating along two independent directions would force both base partials to vanish, contradicting retention of the selected derivative. For the resulting separable factor, the product rule makes B_Z nonzero.
[P9] [proved] deps=[P1],[P6],[P7],[P8]
S: Weighted degree below pd guarantees vertical separability. For total weighted degree at most D<pd, at most floor(D^2/d-D) lines and D-d points per other line need be removed.
P: Zero ordinary Z-derivative forces every positive Z-exponent to be a multiple of p, hence weighted degree at least pd. With iota=0, maximizing h+Delta^2/d-Delta subject to h+Delta<=D and Delta>=d yields D^2/d-D; the point budget is D-d.
[P10] [proved] deps=[P9],KTZ Theorem 1.1 and Lemmas 3.2–6.3
S: Inserting the proved cleanup into the KTZ parameter envelope leaves the benchmark exponent 1/3 and final agreement epsilon/8000 unchanged.
P: KTZ gives D<=1280(sqrt(dp/epsilon)+1) and D<p. The new exceptional-line term is O(1/(epsilon p)), requiring only epsilon^2 p=Omega(1). Both D/p=O(epsilon) and D<k1/2 require sqrt(dp/epsilon)=O(epsilon p), equivalently epsilon^3=Omega(d/p). The quoted conclusion is agreement at least epsilon/8000.
[P11] [proved] deps=[P1]
S: For every campaign pair p,d, a squarefree irreducible polynomial has multiplicity-weighted direction count p(p+1)>pd but no formal graph section.
P: F=Z^p-XY^(p-1) is Eisenstein at X, squarefree, and has weighted degree pd. On direction [a:b], q=ab^(p-1)t gives F(L,Z)=(Z-q)^p. Yet Frobenius p-th powers in F_p[[X,Y]] have all support exponents divisible by p, excluding XY^(p-1).
[P12] [refuted] deps=[P11]
S: The purely inseparable pencil produces a counterexample to affine line-versus-point soundness.
P: For f=xy^(p-1), use X on every line except label the x-axis by zero. Counting gives local agreement (p^2+1)/(p(p+1)); Q=X has global agreement (p(p-1)+1)/p^2.
[P13] [refuted] deps=
S: Concentration of all accepted incidences into a small set of directions alone obstructs global recovery.
P: With f=0, label k complete direction classes by zero and all other lines by one. Local agreement is k/(p+1), but Q=0 agrees at every point.

TITLE Frobenius Purification and the Singular-Lift Bottleneck
THEOREM
For prime p and 101≤d<p: (i) an F_p-irreducible Z-dependent factor of weighted degree δ and Z-degree r with k≥2 absolute factors supports at most (2rδ-dr²)/k²≤δ²/(4d) degree-d graph lines; (ii) for a fixed factor, smooth ramified accepted lifts contribute normalized incidence loss at most 1/(p+1); (iii) if d≤D<2d, a primitive Z-dependent weight-D polynomial is absolutely irreducible and linear in Z, with at most D(D-d) ramified base points; and (iv) for every d≤p-2 there is a minimum-weight D=d+1 primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible interpolant B=XZ+H(Y), whose raw resultant X=0 is a smooth absolutely irreducible plane curve, supporting sp>D fully accepted lines with one ramified point each but no total-degree-d polynomial matching all selected labels. These assertions isolate singular lifted points as the remaining obstruction and do not improve the global soundness exponent.
CONCLUSION
A proved architecture-level correction was obtained, but not a stronger soundness exponent. Geometrically reducible factors can be purged by a sharp Frobenius–resultant bound, and smooth ramified lifts cost at most 1/(p+1) for a fixed factor. The unresolved obstruction is singular accepted lifts: an explicit minimum-weight absolutely irreducible interpolant has a smooth absolutely irreducible resultant curve, supports more than D fully accepted graph lines, and still has no global polynomial root matching all labels. Thus absolute irreducibility and plane-curve point counts alone do not improve the KTZ exponent 1/3.
CHAR
All results assume a prime p and integer 101≤d<p; hence p is odd whenever division by 2 or a nonsquare is used.
- For squarefree B primitive in Z, weighted degree δ<dp implies deg_Z(B)<p and gcd(B,B_Z)=1. The threshold is sharp: Z^p-X has weighted degree dp, is smooth and absolutely irreducible, but B_Z=0.
- The explicit minimum-interpolant construction requires 101≤d≤p-2 and D=d+1<p. At the legal lower boundary p=103,d=101 it has D=102.
- When d=p-1 and d≤D<p, necessarily D=d. Every primitive Z-dependent weighted-degree-D polynomial is cZ+b(X,Y), immediately giving Q=-b/c; the singular construction therefore does not extend to that endpoint.
- No derivative is divided by. The quotient H(c+mt)/t is polynomial division justified by the double root c; its value at t=0 is defined polynomially, not by field inversion.
- For B=XZ+H(Y), the normalized degree-one discriminant is 1 while Res_Z(B,B_Z)=X. Dividing the resultant by the leading coefficient would discard exactly the degree-drop fiber carrying the obstruction.
- The monic stress test Z²+A(X)Z+Y avoids leading-coefficient degeneration. Its ordinary discriminant A²-4Y and the hypersurface are both smooth and absolutely irreducible, yet one graph line has exactly D-d derivative zeros.
- Absolute irreducibility is always geometric irreducibility over the algebraic closure. No extension-field line test, descent, or dimension lift is used.
- All global polynomials Q have total degree at most d. Constant local labels occur only inside ambient instances with d≥101 and do not analyze the excluded d=0 test.
- The incidence normalizations use exactly p(p+1) affine lines and p²(p+1) line-point pairs. The only union loss in the new proofs is Σδ_i²≤D²; there is no Markov, Cauchy–Schwarz, or mixing loss.
OBSTRUCTIONS
Singular accepted lifts may support p distinct graph directions even when the auxiliary polynomial is primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible, vertically separable, and minimum-weight.
- A raw resultant can define a smooth absolutely irreducible plane curve while its lifted fibers contain singular points of the auxiliary hypersurface; plane-curve point counts do not see this directional multiplicity.
- The standard normalized discriminant may divide away a nonconstant leading coefficient and entirely miss degree-dropping fibers.
- The sharp purification loss D²/[4d p(p+1)] becomes O(1/(εp)) at the KTZ interpolation envelope, introducing an ε²p condition if all geometrically reducible factors are discarded.
- The examples obstruct simple-root and deletion architectures but do not upper-bound agreement with every global degree-d polynomial, so they are not counterexamples to KTZ soundness or the fixed-η target.
STEPS
P1 [proved] deps=
S: Squarefree, Z-primitive B with weighted degree below dp satisfies gcd(B,B_Z)=1.
P: If an irreducible C divides squarefree B and B_Z, then C_Z=0. Since deg_Z(C)<p, C is Z-independent, contradicting primitivity. Z^p-X proves sharpness at weighted degree dp.
P2 [proved] deps=
S: Two coprime weighted polynomials support at most s u+r v-drs common degree-d graph lines.
P: The coefficient-degree constraints and isobaric monomials in Res_Z(U,V) give degree at most s u+r v-drs. Every common graph line divides the nonzero resultant.
P3 [proved] deps=P2
S: An F_p-irreducible, geometrically reducible factor supports at most δ²/(dk²) graph lines.
P: Frobenius acts transitively on the k absolute factors. An F_p graph identity for one factor holds for every conjugate; applying P2 to two conjugates gives (2rδ-dr²)/k²≤δ²/(dk²).
P4 [proved] deps=P3
S: All geometrically reducible factors of a primitive weight-D interpolant support at most D²/(4d) graph lines in total, and the constant is sharp.
P: Assign each graph line to an F_p-irreducible factor and use Σδ_i²≤D². The example Z²-νH_0² attains δ²/(4d)=d lines.
P5 [proved] deps=
S: For one fixed factor, smooth ramified accepted lifts contribute at most p² edges and normalized loss 1/(p+1).
P: At B=B_Z=0, differentiating a formal graph identity gives (B_X,B_Y)·v_L=0. At a smooth lift this nonzero functional has one projective kernel direction, hence at most one incident affine graph line per base point.
P6 [proved] deps=
S: In the linear-in-Z regime, the ramified base set has at most D(D-d) points and no graph line is identically ramified.
P: D<2d forces B=aZ+b. Primitivity gives gcd(a,b)=1 and absolute irreducibility. Ramified base points lie in V(a,b), so Bézout gives D(D-d); a common ramified graph line would divide both coefficients.
P7 [proved] deps=P1,P6
S: The explicit B is primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible and vertically separable, while its raw resultant is a smooth absolutely irreducible line.
P: For B=XZ+H(Y), coefficients X and H are coprime, so Gauss's lemma gives absolute irreducibility. Direct differentiation gives B_Z=X and singular lifts (0,c_j,0); direct resultant computation gives Res_Z(B,B_Z)=X.
P8 [proved] deps=P7
S: The explicit uniform-sampling table has selected-line measure s/(p+1) and selected ramified mass s/[p(p+1)].
P: Double roots make H(c_j+mt)/t a degree-at-most-d polynomial. Direct substitution proves the graph identity and agreement with f; counting gives sp selected lines and exactly one B_Z-root on each.
P9 [proved] deps=P8
S: No total-degree-d polynomial matches all selected labels, and B is a minimum-weight graph interpolant.
P: If Q matched every selected line, XQ+H would be a degree-D polynomial vanishing on sp>D lines and hence zero, contradicting X∤H. The same argument excludes every nonzero interpolant below weight D.
P10 [proved] deps=P5
S: Monicity and smooth absolute irreducibility do not improve the pointwise D-d derivative-root bound.
P: For B=Z²+A(X)Z+Y, B_Y=1 and Δ=A²-4Y has Δ_Y=-4. Both are smooth and absolutely irreducible, while B_Z=A on the graph Y=Z=0 has exactly D-d roots.
P11 [proved] deps=P1
S: More than D graph directions and exact per-line ramification saturation can coexist on an absolutely irreducible factor when all bad lifts are singular.
P: For B=Z²-XYg², odd Y-valuation proves absolute irreducibility. Square-slope pencil substitutions give (p-1)/2>D roots, each with d ramified parameters, while the same valuation forbids a polynomial root.
P12 [refuted] deps=P7,P8,P9,P10,P11
S: Absolute irreducibility, a smooth resultant curve, or a smooth monic discriminant cannot replace the simple-lift hypothesis by themselves.
P: The explicit constructions respectively contradict the claimed global-root, projected-curve, and per-line conclusions.
P13 [conditional] deps=P3,P5,P12,Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Lemmas 3.2 and 4.1
S: Within the diagnosed deletion architecture, singular ramification remains the term reproducing the cubic exponent.
P: Substitution of D=O(sqrt(dp/ε)) makes purification O(1/(εp)) and generic singular deletion O(sqrt(d/(εp))). The latter is O(ε) only at cubic scale unless singular incidences receive a new bound.
