TITLE Factorwise Weighted-Polar Accounting and a Sharp Whole-Relation Obstruction
THEOREM
For every prime p and integer 101≤d<p, let B∈F_p[X,Y,Z] be primitive in Z, squarefree, of positive Z-degree, and of (1,1,d)-weighted degree δ<p. Factor B=c∏C_i, put δ_i=wdeg(C_i), r_i=deg_Z(C_i), γ_i=wdeg(C_{i,Z}), and Xi_i=(r_i−1)δ_i+r_iγ_i−dr_i(r_i−1). For any disjoint assignment of labeled affine graph lines T=⊔T_i satisfying C_i(L(t),P_L(t))≡0, at most Xi_i lines of T_i are identically C_{i,Z}-ramified, every other assigned graph has total polar intersection multiplicity at most γ_i, and the normalized accepted-incidence cleanup loss is at most sum_i min{|T_i|,Xi_i}/[p(p+1)]+sum_i |T_i|γ_i/[p²(p+1)]. A connected cleaned factor class of minimum degree k>δ_i with 1/sqrt(p+1)≤k/(4p) yields one total-degree-at-most-d polynomial with global agreement at least k/(4p). Conversely, for every 2d≤D<p there is an explicit legal table and primitive squarefree relation of weighted degree D for which the whole-relation polar loss is exactly (D−d)/(p+1)=τ(D−d)/p, with every intersection simple; for p≥8d this occurs at ε=Theta((d/p)^(1/3)). The construction has a factor Z with derivative one and a degree-zero global decoder, so it is a proof-architecture obstruction rather than a soundness counterexample.
CONCLUSION
A factorwise weighted-resultant and local-intersection-multiplicity lemma is proved. It removes cross-factor collisions from the vertical ramification charge and conditionally yields one global polynomial on any large cleaned factor component. An explicit legal table shows that the unfactored per-line polar bound D−d, including multiplicity accounting, is exactly sharp at the cubic parameter scale. The example is reducible and has a lower-weight factor decoder, so it does not prove sharpness for KTZ's minimum interpolant and does not improve or refute the unconditional 1/3 benchmark.
CHAR
The field is exactly the prime field F_p, with m=2 and 101≤d<p; no extension-field descent or dimension lift is used.
- The algebraic theorem assumes weighted degree δ<p. This implies deg_Z(C_i)<p for every irreducible factor, so a Z-dependent factor has nonzero ordinary Z-derivative. The exact necessary condition is deg_Z(C_i)<p.
- Primitivity in Z excludes every nonconstant Z-independent factor. Absolute irreducibility is neither assumed nor needed.
- No discriminant is divided by. Resultants are used as Sylvester determinants, so specialization remains valid when a leading coefficient vanishes or the specialized Z-degree drops.
- Local intersection multiplicity is ord_t of the pulled-back polar. No factorial, ordinary higher derivative, or Hasse-derivative denominator is used.
- The conditional Newton step divides only by a certified nonzero scalar C_{i,Z}(x,f(x)).
- At d>p/2 and δ<p there can be only one Z-dependent factor and it has Z-degree one. At d=p−1 and δ=d its Z-derivative is a nonzero constant, so the ramification loss is zero.
- The sharpness construction requires 2d≤D<p. It therefore applies at d=101, for example p=211,D=202, but is correctly excluded near d=p.
- All line labels in the constructions are constant but are used under the legal ambient cap d≥101; no degree-zero test parameter is analyzed.
- Global candidates always have total degree at most d; individual degree is never substituted.
OBSTRUCTIONS
Whole-polynomial B_Z charges cross-factor collisions even when the factor carrying the graph is vertically simple.
- The per-line whole-polar bound D−d is exactly sharp, with multiplicity-one roots, and its normalized loss can equal all local agreement at the cubic scale.
- Counting singular lifted points instead of incidences fails under p-fold concurrency.
- No current argument guarantees that a factor class of small weighted polar degree retains minimum degree Omega(εp).
- Even perfect factorwise cleanup leaves KTZ's earlier identity-extension requirement D=O(εp).
- The saturation example is not minimum-weight; proving a minimum-interpolant obstruction remains open.
STEPS
P1 [proved] deps=
S: Every irreducible factor C_i depends on Z and satisfies gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1.
P: Primitivity excludes every Z-independent irreducible factor. Since δ<p, each positive Z-degree is below p, so formal differentiation is nonzero; irreducibility and lower Z-degree give coprimality with the derivative.
P2 [proved] deps=
S: The weighted resultant has base degree at most su+rv−drs.
P: Sylvester multihomogeneity uses s coefficients of U and r coefficients of V with aggregate Z-index rs. The coefficient degree bounds u−di and v−dj give deg Res_Z(U,V)≤su+rv−drs.
P3 [proved] deps=P1,P2
S: The assigned factor C_i has at most Xi_i identically ramified graph lines, counted with resultant multiplicity.
P: Apply P2 to C_i and C_{i,Z}. Every common graph line supplies a distinct affine-linear factor of the nonzero resultant; summing its divisorial orders is bounded by the resultant degree Xi_i.
P4 [proved] deps=P1
S: Every nonexceptional assigned graph has total local polar intersection multiplicity at most γ_i.
P: The nonzero pullback C_{i,Z}(L(t),P_L(t)) has degree at most γ_i, so the sum of its root orders is at most γ_i.
P5 [proved] deps=P3,P4
S: The factorwise ramified-incidence measure obeys the exact and coarse bounds in Theorem A.
P: Exceptional lines contribute at most p incidences and other lines at most γ_i. Normalization and the inequalities γ_i≤δ_i−d and Xi_i≤δ_i²/d−δ_i give both displayed cleanup bounds.
P6 [conditional] deps=P1,P5,KTZ Lemmas 2.1, 2.7, and 6.1–6.3
S: A connected cleaned factor class satisfying the stated degree and mixing hypotheses yields one total-degree-at-most-d polynomial with agreement at least k/(4p).
P: A simple factor root at one point lifts uniquely from more than δ_i incident graph lines and propagates through the connected factor component. The affine incidence mixing inequality gives point density at least k/(4p).
P7 [proved] deps=
S: The explicit table in Theorem C has local agreement (D−d)/(p+1).
P: For e=D−d, choose squarefree H of degree e and B=Z(Z−H). The strip-valued f, zero nonvertical labels, and vertical label two give exactly e accepted points on each of p² nonvertical lines.
P8 [proved] deps=P7
S: The whole-relation polar loss equals τ(D−d)/p exactly, whereas the factorwise loss is zero.
P: On every supported graph B_Z=−H(u+vt), which has exactly D−d simple roots. These are exactly the accepted points, while assignment to C=Z has derivative one.
P9 [proved] deps=P7,P8
S: The sharpness construction realizes the cubic parameter balance.
P: With e=ceil((dp²)^(1/3)) and p≥8d, elementary inequalities give D<p, ε=Theta((d/p)^(1/3)), D=Theta(εp), and D=Theta(sqrt(dp/ε)).
P10 [refuted] deps=P7,P8
S: The sharpness construction forces KTZ's minimum-weight interpolant to incur the whole-relation polar loss.
P: The lower-weight polynomial Z already vanishes on all selected graphs and has vertical derivative one.
P11 [refuted] deps=P1
S: Inseparability or concentrated good directions refute the factorwise theorem in the campaign regime.
P: Z^p−X violates δ<p, while concentration in k directions has the perfect decoder Q=0.

TITLE Sharp Incidence-Only Losses in Bivariate Prime-Field Line–Point Agreement
THEOREM
Let p be prime, let 101≤d<p, fix m=2, and use uniform affine point-line sampling. There exist f:F_p²→F_p and degree-exactly-d line labels such that ε=d/p, every affine line has exactly d accepted points, every point has accepted degree d or d+1, exactly pd points have degree d+1, and every line supports acceptance. For every θ∈(0,1], the minimum total nonnegative line weight that gives every accepted incidence θ capped coverage through other accepted lines lies between θp(p+1)/(d+1) and θp(p+1)/(d−1); hence every integral such family has size Ω(p/ε). Nevertheless Q=0 has global agreement one. Moreover, for every valid table of agreement ε, the probability Γ₂ that two uniformly chosen distinct lines through a uniform point both accept there satisfies Γ₂=ε²−ε(1−ε)/p+Var_x(deg_acc(x))/[p(p+1)], and the lower bound is attained by accepting exactly k direction classes, ε=k/(p+1).
CONCLUSION
No unconditional exponent beyond 1/3 is proved. The new result sharply rules out two incidence-blind improvements: compatible two-line collisions can be only Θ(ε²), and a universal family reaching a constant fraction of every accepted line can require Θ(p/ε) fractional weight even on an exactly almost-regular, fully supported, legal table. Because the extremal tables have the obvious decoder Q=0, the conclusion is a rigorous sparsify-or-decode obstruction, not a counterexample to soundness. KTZ already uses the exact affine Vinh estimate; improving toward exponent 1/2 must attack covering/interpolation or decode the obstructing case, while any exponent above 1/2 also needs a replacement for Vinh-only component recovery in low-d regimes.
CHAR
The field is exactly the prime field F_p and the ambient degree satisfies 101≤d<p throughout.
- Each constructed line label is a formal degree-exactly-d product of d distinct linear factors. Since d<p, its accepted points are exactly its formal roots; no polynomial-function aliasing occurs.
- The construction uses no derivative division, discriminant, resultant, irreducibility assumption, interpolation multiplicity, factorial, Markov inequality, union bound, or probabilistic existence argument.
- The collision lemma uses one exact variance identity; its ε² loss is attained when accepted point degrees are constant.
- Fixed affine parameterizations handle vertical lines without slope division.
- The concentrated-direction equality example uses constant labels only as legal labels under the ambient cap d>100; it does not analyze a degree-zero test.
- At d=p−1 the repair graph has degree p+1−d=2, so the Hall argument remains valid. At d=101 it is unchanged.
- Stevens–de Zeeuw is used only with its positive-characteristic size hypotheses stated explicitly. Its results concern distinct unweighted lines and do not automatically apply to fractional or repeated-line selectors.
OBSTRUCTIONS
The exact two-line compatibility energy can be only ε²−O(ε/p); no incidence convexity argument can force a better power from ε alone.
- A universal fractional family reaching a constant fraction of every accepted line can require Θ(p/ε) total weight, even on a fully supported, exactly almost-regular legal table.
- The point and line projections of the regular table are the entire affine plane and all affine lines. Vinh's main term is exact on these projections, so unweighted incidence bounds discard the acceptance information.
- Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorem 3 requires comparable distinct sets and a characteristic-size condition; the component-size exponent it supplies becomes linear only outside that range.
- Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorem 4 additionally requires a Cartesian point set and likewise has no parameter overlap where its consequence supplies linear recovery.
- For fixed legal d and a candidate soundness exponent above 1/2, the KTZ/Vinh mixing requirement ε=Ω(p^−1/2) fails.
- The extremal incidence examples are easy to decode, so none refutes the soundness target; they force a dichotomy rather than establish impossibility.
STEPS
P1 [proved] deps=Hall's marriage theorem, proved in the step by regular degree counting
S: The affine point-line incidence graph admits a p+1-color decomposition in which every point sees all colors and each line misses one color.
P: Add p dummy point vertices, connecting dummy b to the p finite-slope lines of intercept b and the vertical line x=b. The resulting balanced graph is (p+1)-regular and decomposes iteratively into p+1 perfect matchings. Restriction gives an affine incidence edge-coloring in which every point sees every color and every line misses exactly one.
P2 [proved] deps=P1,Hall's marriage theorem
S: There is a spanning affine incidence subgraph with every line of degree d and every point of degree d or d+1.
P: Selecting d colors gives point degree d and line degree d or d−1, with exactly pd deficient lines. In the unselected graph from deficient lines, every left degree is r=p+1−d and every right degree is at most r, so Hall supplies a matching saturating all deficient lines. Adding it gives exact line degree d and point degree d or d+1.
P3 [proved] deps=P2
S: The regular incidence subgraph is realized by a legal degree-d line-versus-point table.
P: Set f=0 and, on each fixed line parameterization, take the product of t−a over the d selected incidence parameters. Since d<p, this degree-d polynomial vanishes exactly on the selected points. Counting edges gives ε=d/p, and Q=0 agrees globally everywhere.
P4 [proved] deps=P2,P3
S: Every fractional family reaching a θ-fraction of every line through other accepted lines has weight at least θp(p+1)/(d+1).
P: Replace target-excluded loads by the larger common capped load v_x. Double counting gives θd p(p+1)≤Σ_x a_xv_x≤(d+1)Σ_xv_x≤d(d+1)Σ_Lλ_L.
P5 [proved] deps=P2,P3
S: The other-line fractional-cover lower bound is tight within factor (d+1)/(d−1).
P: Assign every line weight θ/(d−1). At every accepted incidence, at least d−1 other accepted lines contribute, so its capped other-line load is at least θ. Total weight is θp(p+1)/(d−1).
P6 [proved] deps=
S: The ordered distinct-line joint-acceptance probability equals ε²−ε(1−ε)/p+Var(a_x)/[p(p+1)].
P: At point x there are exactly a_x(a_x−1) ordered pairs of distinct accepted incident lines. Averaging and expanding E[a_x²] as E[a_x]²+Var(a_x), with E[a_x]=(p+1)ε, gives the exact formula.
P7 [proved] deps=P6
S: The collision lower bound is attained by concentrated good directions for every ε=k/(p+1).
P: Accept all incidences on k direction classes using zero labels and reject all others using constant-one labels, with f=0. Every point has accepted degree k, so the variance vanishes and Γ₂=k(k−1)/[p(p+1)].
P8 [proved] deps=Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Revision 1, Lemmas 3.2 and 4.1–4.3
S: The KTZ cubic threshold is reconstructed from covering, interpolation, and root supply.
P: The source gives |R|=O(p/ε), weighted degree D=O(sqrt(d|R|)), and requires D=O(εp). Squaring yields ε³=Ω(d/p). The probabilistic selector losses are constants and KTZ does not claim simultaneous Θ-saturation.
P9 [proved] deps=Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Revision 1, Lemmas 2.1 and 6.2
S: Vinh-only component recovery has a method barrier at exponent 1/2 in the legal lower-boundary regime.
P: The component argument requires (p+1)^−1/2≤ε/8000. For d=101 and ε=C(101/p)^α with fixed α>1/2, εsqrt(p) tends to zero, so this Vinh-based recovery step cannot yield linear component size.
P10 [proved] deps=Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorems 3 and 4
S: The quoted stronger prime-field incidence bounds do not supply linear component recovery within their own characteristic ranges.
P: For balanced supports, kN≪N^(22/15) yields N≫k^(15/7); making this as large as kp forces parameters outside N≪p^(15/11). In the balanced Cartesian case, kN≪N^(11/8)+N yields N≫k^(8/3); making this as large as kp forces parameters outside N≪p^(4/3).
P11 [refuted] deps=P4,P5,P6,P7
S: Acceptance density alone always yields either more than quadratic collision energy or an o(p/ε)-weight universal reaching family.
P: The regular carrier rules out a universally smaller reaching family, and the direction table attains the collision square. Both have Q=0, so they refute only the incidence-only shortcut, not soundness.

researcher-0005:[P3].1 Weighted Resultants status=proved
STATEMENT
Let $U,V\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be nonzero and coprime, with

$$u=\operatorname{wdeg}U,\quad v=\operatorname{wdeg}V,\quad r=\deg_ZU,\quad s=\deg_ZV,$$

under the weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a set of distinct affine lines in $\mathbb F_p^2$, each equipped with one polynomial $q_L$ of degree at most $d$, such that

$$U(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv V(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$$

in $\mathbb F_p[t]$. Then

$$|\mathcal T|\le su+rv-drs\le\frac{uv}{d}.$$
PROOF
Assume first that both $Z$-degrees are positive. Since $U$ and $V$ are coprime, their Sylvester resultant $R(X,Y)=\operatorname{Res}_Z(U,V)$ is nonzero. Every determinant term contains $s$ coefficients of $U$ and $r$ coefficients of $V$. Weighted homogeneity therefore bounds its ordinary degree in $X,Y$ by

$$\deg R\le su+rv-drs.$$

The further inequality follows from

$$(u-dr)(v-ds)\ge0,$$

which is equivalent to $su+rv-drs\le uv/d$.

For every $L\in\mathcal T$, specialization of the Sylvester determinant gives $R(L(t))\equiv0$, because the two specialized polynomials have the common root $q_L(t)$. Thus the affine linear equation defining $L$ divides $R$. Distinct underlying lines give distinct linear factors, and a nonzero bivariate polynomial has no more distinct affine-line factors than its total degree. If one $Z$-degree is zero, the same conclusion follows directly by counting the affine-line factors of the corresponding polynomial in $X,Y$. No leading coefficient is divided out.
DEPS 

researcher-0005:[P6].1 Exceptional Lines status=proved
STATEMENT
Let

$$B=c\prod_{i=1}^rC_i,
\qquad \delta_i:=\operatorname{wdeg}C_i,
\qquad \Delta:=\sum_i\delta_i,$$

where the $C_i$ are distinct irreducible $Z$-dependent factors, and let

$$\iota:=\operatorname{wdeg}\gcd(B,B_Z)=\sum_{C_{i,Z}=0}\delta_i.$$

For $C_{i,Z}\ne0$, set $D_iC_i:=C_{i,Z}$ and $\omega_i:=d$. For $C_{i,Z}=0$, at least one member of $\{C_{i,X},C_{i,Y}\}$ is nonzero; choose one as $D_iC_i$ and set $\omega_i:=1$.

Among the non-content graph lines supplied by [P5], call a line exceptional if either at least two distinct factors vanish identically on it, or exactly one factor $C_i$ vanishes identically and $D_iC_i$ also vanishes identically. The number $N_{\mathrm{exc}}$ of exceptional lines satisfies

$$N_{\mathrm{exc}}
\le
\sum_i\left\lfloor\frac{\delta_i(\delta_i-\omega_i)}{d}\right\rfloor
+
\sum_{i<j}\left\lfloor\frac{\delta_i\delta_j}{d}\right\rfloor$$

and

$$N_{\mathrm{exc}}
\le
\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^2-d\Delta+(d-1)\iota}{d}\right\rfloor.$$
PROOF
If two distinct factors $C_i$ and $C_j$ vanish identically on the same graph line, the weighted resultant bound gives at most

$$\left\lfloor\frac{\delta_i\delta_j}{d}\right\rfloor$$

such lines. If the unique factor is $C_i$ and its selected derivative also vanishes identically, the same bound gives at most

$$\left\lfloor\frac{\delta_i(\delta_i-\omega_i)}{d}\right\rfloor$$

such lines, because $\operatorname{wdeg}(D_iC_i)\le\delta_i-\omega_i$ and $C_i$ is coprime to its selected nonzero derivative.

For an inseparable irreducible factor, at least one of $C_{i,X}$ and $C_{i,Y}$ is nonzero. Otherwise all three ordinary partial derivatives would vanish. Every exponent would then be divisible by $p$, and perfectness of $\mathbb F_p$ would express $C_i$ as a nontrivial $p$-th power, contradicting irreducibility.

Summing the factor-pair and factor-derivative bounds gives the first inequality by a deterministic union bound. Finally,

$$\sum_i\omega_i\delta_i=d(\Delta-\iota)+\iota$$

and

$$\sum_i\delta_i^2+\sum_{i<j}\delta_i\delta_j\le\Delta^2.$$

Substitution into the first bound, followed by integrality of $N_{\mathrm{exc}}$, gives the second bound.
DEPS [P3],[P5]

researcher-0005:[P8].1 Simple Intersections status=proved
STATEMENT
In the incidence system retained after [P7], let $(x,y,z)\in\mathbb F_p^3$ be incident to at least two distinct retained graph lines. Then

$$B(x,y,z)=0
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
B_Z(x,y,z)\ne0.$$
PROOF
Suppose two distinct retained graph lines meet at $(x,y,z)$. The deletion of roots of every other factor forces the two lines to have the same unique type $i$: if their types differed, the factor belonging to either line would vanish at the common point on the other line, and that point would have been deleted.

Assume that $C_{i,Z}=0$. Differentiating the two formal graph identities along their respective direction vectors $(a,b)$ gives

$$C_{i,X}(x,y,z)a+C_{i,Y}(x,y,z)b=0.$$

The two underlying affine lines are distinct and pass through the same point, so their direction vectors are independent. The two equations force

$$C_{i,X}(x,y,z)=C_{i,Y}(x,y,z)=0,$$

contradicting retention of the chosen nonzero base derivative. Hence $C_i$ is separable.

Retention also gives $C_{i,Z}(x,y,z)\ne0$ and $C_j(x,y,z)\ne0$ for every $j\ne i$. Since $C_i(x,y,z)=0$, the product rule reduces to

$$B_Z(x,y,z)=c\,C_{i,Z}(x,y,z)\prod_{j\ne i}C_j(x,y,z)\ne0.$$
DEPS [P7]

researcher-0005:[P9].1 Automatic Separability status=proved
STATEMENT
Let $B=c\prod_iC_i$ be squarefree and $Z$-dependent, with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}B$ and $\iota=\operatorname{wdeg}\gcd(B,B_Z)$. If

$$\Delta<pd,$$

then

$$\iota=0
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$

If additionally $A=HB$, $h=\deg H$, $a=h+\Delta$, and

$$a\le D<pd,$$

then the cleanup removes at most

$$\left\lfloor\frac{D^2}{d}-D\right\rfloor$$

content and exceptional lines in total and at most $D-d$ parameter values from every other line. Every retained point incident to at least two retained graph lines satisfies $B_Z\ne0$.
PROOF
If an irreducible $Z$-dependent factor $C_i$ satisfies $C_{i,Z}=0$, every positive $Z$-exponent in $C_i$ is divisible by $p$. Such an exponent is therefore at least $p$, and the assigned weight of the corresponding $Z$-power is at least $pd$. Hence $\delta_i\ge pd$. The inequality $\Delta<pd$ excludes every such factor, so $\iota=0$ and $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$.

With $\iota=0$, the content and exceptional-line bounds give at most

$$h+\frac{\Delta^2}{d}-\Delta$$

lines before taking the integer floor. Since $B$ is $Z$-dependent, $\Delta\ge d$. For fixed $a=h+\Delta$, this convex expression is maximized at $h=0$ and $\Delta=a$; the comparison of the two endpoints is

$$\frac{a^2}{d}-a-(a-d)=\frac{(a-d)^2}{d}\ge0.$$

The resulting expression $a^2/d-a$ is nondecreasing for $a\ge d$, so $a\le D$ gives the bound $\lfloor D^2/d-D\rfloor$. Every factor is separable, and the per-line deletion bound is $\Delta-d\le D-d$. The final simple-point conclusion follows from [P8].
DEPS [P1],[P6],[P7],[P8]

researcher-0006:P1.1 Vertical separability status=proved
STATEMENT
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$. If $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ is squarefree, primitive as a polynomial in $Z$, and satisfies $\operatorname{wdeg}B<dp$, then $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$.
PROOF
Put $\delta=\operatorname{wdeg}B$. Then $\deg_ZB\le\lfloor\delta/d\rfloor<p$. Suppose an irreducible polynomial $C$ divides both $B$ and $B_Z$. Writing $B=CE$, squarefreeness gives $C\nmid E$, while differentiation modulo $C$ gives $C\mid C_ZE$. Hence $C\mid C_Z$, so $C_Z=0$. In characteristic $p$, every positive $Z$-exponent of $C$ is therefore divisible by $p$. Since $\deg_ZC<p$, the polynomial $C$ is independent of $Z$, contradicting the primitivity of $B$. Thus $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$.

The strict threshold is sharp: $Z^p-X$ is primitive, squarefree, smooth, and absolutely irreducible, has weighted degree $dp$, and has zero $Z$-derivative.
DEPS 

researcher-0006:P2.1 Weighted resultant bound status=proved
STATEMENT
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $U,V\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be nonzero and coprime, with $u=\operatorname{wdeg}U$, $v=\operatorname{wdeg}V$, $r=\deg_ZU$, and $s=\deg_ZV$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a family of distinct affine lines with parametrizations $L(t)$ and labels $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$ such that $U(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv V(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ for every $L\in\mathcal T$. Then $|\mathcal T|\le su+rv-drs$.
PROOF
Write $U=\sum_iU_iZ^i$ and $V=\sum_jV_jZ^j$. The weighted-degree constraints give $\deg U_i\le u-di$ and $\deg V_j\le v-dj$. The nonzero resultant $R=\operatorname{Res}_Z(U,V)$ is homogeneous of degree $s$ in the coefficients of $U$ and degree $r$ in those of $V$. Every resultant monomial has total vertical-index weight $rs$, so its ordinary $(X,Y)$-degree is at most $su+rv-drs$. Hence $\deg R\le su+rv-drs$.

For each $L\in\mathcal T$, the specialized polynomials $U(L(t),Z)$ and $V(L(t),Z)$ have the common factor $Z-P_L(t)$, so $R(L(t))\equiv0$. The affine linear equation of $L$ therefore divides $R$. Distinct lines give distinct factors, proving the bound. No leading coefficient is divided out.
DEPS 

researcher-0006:P5.1 Smooth ramification sparsity status=proved
STATEMENT
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$, let $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and let $\mathcal T$ be a family of affine lines parametrized by $L(t)$ with labels $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$ satisfying $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. Let $\mathcal E$ be the set of pairs $(L,\tau)$ satisfying $P_L(\tau)=f(L(\tau))$ and $B_Z(L(\tau),P_L(\tau))=0$. If every lift $(L(\tau),P_L(\tau))$ belonging to $\mathcal E$ is smooth on $B=0$, then $|\mathcal E|\le p^2$, and $|\mathcal E|/[p^2(p+1)]\le1/(p+1)$.
PROOF
Fix $(L,\tau)\in\mathcal E$, write $w=(L(\tau),f(L(\tau)))$, and let $v_L=(v_{L,1},v_{L,2})$ be the direction of $L$. Differentiating the formal graph identity gives $B_X(w)v_{L,1}+B_Y(w)v_{L,2}+B_Z(w)P_L'(\tau)=0$. Ramification gives $B_Z(w)=0$, while smoothness gives $(B_X(w),B_Y(w))\ne(0,0)$. Thus $v_L$ lies in the unique projective kernel direction of the nonzero functional $(B_X(w),B_Y(w))$.

All accepted incidences above a fixed base point have the same lift $(x,f(x))$. Hence that point permits at most one affine graph line. Summing over the $p^2$ base points gives $|\mathcal E|\le p^2$. Division by the ambient $p^2(p+1)$ incidences gives the normalized bound. No derivative is divided by.
DEPS 

researcher-0006:P6.1 Linear vertical regime status=proved
STATEMENT
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $d\le D<2d$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, $Z$-dependent, and satisfy $\operatorname{wdeg}B\le D$. Then $|\{(x,y)\in\mathbb F_p^2:\exists z\in\mathbb F_p,\ B(x,y,z)=B_Z(x,y,z)=0\}|\le D(D-d)$, and there is no affine-line parametrization $L(t)$ with a degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $P(t)$ satisfying both $B(L(t),P(t))\equiv0$ and $B_Z(L(t),P(t))\equiv0$.
PROOF
Since $D<2d$ and $B$ is $Z$-dependent, $B=a(X,Y)Z+b(X,Y)$ with $\deg a\le D-d$ and $\deg b\le D$. Primitivity gives $\gcd(a,b)=1$, and Gauss's lemma makes this primitive linear polynomial absolutely irreducible.

Because $B_Z=a$, a base point admits a ramified lift exactly when $a=b=0$. Bézout's theorem therefore bounds the ramified base set by $\deg a\deg b\le D(D-d)$. Deleting all these base vertices removes normalized incidence mass at most $D(D-d)/p^2$.

If a graph line were identically ramified, then $a(L(t))\equiv0$; substituting into $B(L(t),P(t))\equiv0$ gives $b(L(t))\equiv0$. The line equation would divide both $a$ and $b$, contradicting their coprimality.
DEPS 

researcher-0006:P12.1 Simplicity substitutes status=refuted
STATEMENT
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$. The following assertions hold:

1. If an absolutely irreducible graph interpolant of weighted degree at most $D<p$ supports more than $D$ distinct affine graph lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels, then it has a polynomial root $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $B(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0$, without any simple-common-lift hypothesis.

2. Smooth absolute irreducibility of the raw-resultant curve $\operatorname{Res}_Z(B,B_Z)=0$ controls the number of graph directions meeting a ramified lifted point.

3. If $2d\le D<p$, a graph interpolant is monic in $Z$, and its $Z$-discriminant is smooth and absolutely irreducible, then every degree-at-most-$d$ graph line contains $o(D-d)$ parameters at which $B_Z$ vanishes.
PROOF
Entries [P7]–[P9] give an absolutely irreducible interpolant of weight $D$ supporting $sp>D$ graph lines, but [P9] rules out a total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial restricting to all their labels; the equation $XQ+H=0$ also rules out a polynomial root. Entry [P11] supplies another absolutely irreducible example with more than $D$ graph directions and no polynomial root. These examples refute the first assertion.

For [P7] and [P8], $\operatorname{Res}_Z(B,B_Z)=X$ is a smooth absolutely irreducible line. Nevertheless, for each $j$, all $p$ lines indexed by $m\in\mathbb F_p$ pass through the same ramified lift $(0,c_j,0)$. This refutes the second assertion.

In [P10], the interpolant is monic and both its hypersurface and its discriminant are smooth and absolutely irreducible, while the displayed graph line contains exactly $D-d$ ramified parameters. This is not $o(D-d)$ and refutes the third assertion.
DEPS P7,P8,P9,P10,P11

researcher-0006:P13.1 Singular deletion scale status=conditional
STATEMENT
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $0<\epsilon\le1$. Suppose the interpolation weight satisfies $D=O(\sqrt{dp/\epsilon})$. Suppose a deletion argument assigns supported graph lines to fixed $\mathbb F_p$-irreducible factors, deletes graph lines supported on geometrically reducible factors using [P3], applies [P5] only to ramified lifts known to be smooth on their assigned factor, and charges every remaining singular ramified incidence by the per-line budget $D/p$. Then the normalized purification loss is $O(1/(\epsilon p))$, the normalized smooth-ramification loss for each fixed assigned factor is at most $1/(p+1)$, and the singular-ramification loss is $O(D/p)=O(\sqrt{d/(\epsilon p)})$. Requiring the last loss to be $O(\epsilon)$ imposes $\epsilon^3=\Omega(d/p)$.
PROOF
Applying [P3] to the geometrically reducible factors of weights $\delta_i$ and summing gives at most $\sum_i\delta_i^2/(4d)\le D^2/(4d)$ removed graph lines. After uniform-incidence normalization, their cost is at most $D^2/[4dp(p+1)]=O(1/(\epsilon p))$ under the stated envelope. Entry [P5] bounds the smooth ramified incidences for a fixed assigned factor by $p^2$, hence by normalized mass $1/(p+1)$. These estimates require the stated factor assignment and lifted-point smoothness; [P12] shows that absolute irreducibility alone does not supply the latter.

If the remaining singular incidences are deleted using the per-line budget, their normalized cost is $O(D/p)=O(\sqrt{d/(\epsilon p)})$. Requiring this quantity to be $O(\epsilon)$ gives $\sqrt{d/(\epsilon p)}=O(\epsilon)$, which is equivalent, up to absolute constants, to $\epsilon^3=\Omega(d/p)$. The calculation is conditional on this deletion architecture and does not give a lower bound for methods that globally control singular accepted lifts.
DEPS P3,P5,P12,Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Lemmas 3.2 and 4.1

researcher-0007:P1.1 Factor separability status=proved
STATEMENT
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, squarefree, of positive $Z$-degree and weighted degree $\delta<p$, and write

$$
B=c\prod_{i=1}^{s}C_i,
\qquad c\in\mathbb F_p^\times,
$$

where the $C_i$ are distinct irreducible polynomials. For

$$
\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),
\qquad
r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),
$$

every $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$ satisfies

$$
r_i\ge1
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1.
$$
PROOF
If some $C_i$ were independent of $Z$, then $C_i\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ would divide every coefficient of $B$ viewed as a polynomial in $Z$, contradicting primitivity. Hence $r_i>0$.

The weighted-degree bound gives

$$
d r_i\le \delta_i\le \delta<p,
$$

so $1\le r_i<p$. The derivative of the leading $Z$-term is therefore nonzero, and

$$
C_{i,Z}\ne0,
\qquad
\deg_Z(C_{i,Z})=r_i-1<r_i.
$$

Since $C_i$ is irreducible, any nonconstant common divisor of $C_i$ and $C_{i,Z}$ would be associated to $C_i$, which is impossible because $C_{i,Z}$ has smaller $Z$-degree. Thus $\gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1$. Absolute irreducibility is not used.
DEPS 

researcher-0007:P2.1 Weighted resultant degree status=proved
STATEMENT
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let

$$
U=\sum_{a=0}^{r}u_a(X,Y)Z^a,
\qquad
V=\sum_{b=0}^{s}v_b(X,Y)Z^b
$$

be nonzero polynomials in $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ satisfying

$$
\deg_Z(U)=r,
\qquad
\deg_Z(V)=s,
\qquad
\operatorname{wdeg}(U)=u,
\qquad
\operatorname{wdeg}(V)=v.
$$

With the convention $\deg_{X,Y}(0)=-\infty$,

$$
\deg_{X,Y}\operatorname{Res}_Z(U,V)\le su+rv-drs.
$$
PROOF
Weighted degree gives

$$
\deg_{X,Y}(u_a)\le u-da,
\qquad
\deg_{X,Y}(v_b)\le v-db
$$

for every nonzero coefficient. Every monomial in the Sylvester determinant contains $s$ coefficients of $U$ and $r$ coefficients of $V$, and the aggregate coefficient-index sum is $rs$. Its base degree is consequently at most

$$
su+rv-drs.
$$

Taking the sum of the determinant monomials cannot increase this bound. This calculation uses the Sylvester determinant directly and divides by no leading coefficient. If one $Z$-degree is $0$, the formula is the direct degree bound for the corresponding base polynomial. Because the determinant is polynomial in the coefficients, the calculation remains valid under specialization even when a leading coefficient vanishes or the specialized $Z$-degree drops.
DEPS 

researcher-0007:P3.1 Exceptional graph lines status=proved
STATEMENT
Assume the hypotheses and notation of researcher-0007:P1.1. Fix $i$ and put

$$
\gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),
\qquad
R_i=\operatorname{Res}_Z(C_i,C_{i,Z}),
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).
$$

Let $\mathcal T_i$ be a family indexed by distinct affine lines with equations $\ell_L(X,Y)=0$. For each $L\in\mathcal T_i$, let $L(t)=(x_L(t),y_L(t))$ be an affine-linear parametrization and let $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ satisfy $\deg P_L\le d$ and

$$
C_i(x_L(t),y_L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.
$$

Define

$$
\mathcal E_i=
\left\{L\in\mathcal T_i:
C_{i,Z}(x_L(t),y_L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\right\}
$$

and, for $L\in\mathcal E_i$,

$$
\nu_L=\operatorname{ord}_{\ell_L}(R_i).
$$

Then

$$
R_i\ne0,
\qquad
\deg_{X,Y}(R_i)\le\Xi_i,
\qquad
\sum_{L\in\mathcal E_i}\nu_L\le\Xi_i.
$$

In particular,

$$
|\mathcal E_i|\le\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}.
$$
PROOF
Factor separability gives $\gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1$, so the $Z$-resultant $R_i$ is nonzero. Moreover,

$$
\deg_Z(C_i)=r_i,
\qquad
\deg_Z(C_{i,Z})=r_i-1.
$$

The weighted resultant bound applied to $C_i$ and $C_{i,Z}$ gives

$$
\deg_{X,Y}(R_i)
\le (r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1)
=\Xi_i.
$$

For every $L\in\mathcal E_i$, both pullbacks vanish identically. Specializing the Sylvester determinant to the base line therefore gives

$$
R_i(x_L(t),y_L(t))\equiv0,
$$

including when the specialized $Z$-degree drops. Hence $\ell_L$ divides $R_i$ and $\nu_L\ge1$. The base lines are distinct, so their affine-linear factors are pairwise nonassociate. Consequently,

$$
\sum_{L\in\mathcal E_i}\nu_L
\le \deg_{X,Y}(R_i)
\le\Xi_i.
$$

The cardinality bound follows from $\nu_L\ge1$.
DEPS researcher-0007:P1.1,researcher-0007:P2.1

researcher-0007:P4.1 Finite polar intersections status=proved
STATEMENT
Let the objects satisfy the hypotheses of researcher-0007:P3.1. For $L\in\mathcal T_i\setminus\mathcal E_i$, set

$$
G_{i,L}(t)=C_{i,Z}(x_L(t),y_L(t),P_L(t)).
$$

Then $G_{i,L}\ne0$ and

$$
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb F_p}
\operatorname{ord}_{t=\alpha}G_{i,L}(t)
\le\gamma_i.
$$
PROOF
Every monomial of $C_{i,Z}$ has weighted degree at most $\gamma_i$. Under the substitutions

$$
X=x_L(t),
\qquad
Y=y_L(t),
\qquad
Z=P_L(t),
$$

the degrees of the $X$- and $Y$-factors are at most their exponents, while the degree contributed by a $Z$-factor is at most $d$ times its exponent. Hence

$$
\deg G_{i,L}\le\gamma_i.
$$

The sum of the orders of any collection of roots of a nonzero univariate polynomial is at most its degree, proving the stated inequality. For any point table $f$, accepted incidences at which $C_{i,Z}(L(t),f(L(t)))=0$ are a subset of these roots because acceptance gives $P_L(t)=f(L(t))$. Thus the orders provide the full local polar-intersection charge. No factorial, ordinary higher derivative, or Hasse-derivative denominator is used.
DEPS researcher-0007:P1.1

researcher-0007:P5.1 Factorwise cleanup bounds status=proved
STATEMENT
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, squarefree, of positive $Z$-degree and weighted degree $\delta<p$, with

$$
B=c\prod_{i=1}^{s}C_i.
$$

For each $i$, put

$$
\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),
\quad
r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),
\quad
\gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).
$$

Let $\mathcal T=\bigsqcup_i\mathcal T_i$ be a disjoint assignment of distinct affine lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels $P_L$, satisfying

$$
C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\qquad(L\in\mathcal T_i).
$$

For each $i$, let

$$
e_i=
\left|
\left\{L\in\mathcal T_i:
C_{i,Z}(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\right\}
\right|.
$$

For a point table $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, let $i(L)=i$ when $L\in\mathcal T_i$, and define

$$
\mathcal R=
\left\{(L,t):
\begin{array}{l}
L\in\mathcal T,\ t\in\mathbb F_p,\\
P_L(t)=f(L(t)),\\
C_{i(L),Z}(L(t),f(L(t)))=0
\end{array}
\right\}.
$$

Then

$$
e_i\le\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\},
$$

$$
|\mathcal R|
\le
\sum_i\left(pe_i+(|\mathcal T_i|-e_i)\gamma_i\right),
$$

and

$$
\frac{|\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}
\le
\sum_i\frac{\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}}{p(p+1)}
+
\sum_i\frac{|\mathcal T_i|\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}.
$$

Moreover,

$$
\gamma_i\le\delta_i-d
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i\le(2r_i-1)\delta_i-dr_i^2
\le\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i.
$$

If

$$
\tau_i=\frac{|\mathcal T_i|}{p(p+1)},
\qquad
\tau=\sum_i\tau_i>0,
\qquad
\bar\delta=\frac{\sum_i\tau_i\delta_i}{\tau},
$$

then

$$
\frac{|\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}
\le
\frac{\delta^2/d-\delta}{p(p+1)}
+
\frac{\tau(\bar\delta-d)}p.
$$
PROOF
The exceptional graph-line bound gives

$$
e_i\le\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}.
$$

An exceptional line has $p$ parameter values and therefore contributes at most $p$ accepted incidences. On a nonexceptional line, acceptance identifies $f(L(t))$ with $P_L(t)$, so the finite polar-intersection bound limits the ramified accepted incidences, with multiplicity, by $\gamma_i$. Thus

$$
|\mathcal R|
\le
\sum_i\left(pe_i+(|\mathcal T_i|-e_i)\gamma_i\right).
$$

Bounding $e_i$ by $\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}$, discarding the nonpositive terms $-e_i\gamma_i$, and dividing by $p^2(p+1)$ gives the normalized bound.

Formal differentiation with respect to $Z$ lowers weighted degree by at least $d$, so

$$
\gamma_i\le\delta_i-d.
$$

Substitution into $\Xi_i$ gives

$$
\begin{aligned}
\Xi_i
&\le(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i(\delta_i-d)-dr_i(r_i-1)\\
&=(2r_i-1)\delta_i-dr_i^2\\
&=\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i-
\frac{(\delta_i-dr_i)^2}{d}\\
&\le\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i.
\end{aligned}
$$

Weighted degree is additive under products, so

$$
\sum_i\delta_i=\delta,
\qquad
\sum_i\delta_i^2\le\delta^2.
$$

It follows that

$$
\sum_i\Xi_i\le\frac{\delta^2}{d}-\delta.
$$

Finally,

$$
\sum_i\frac{|\mathcal T_i|\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}
=\frac1p\sum_i\tau_i\gamma_i
\le\frac1p\sum_i\tau_i(\delta_i-d)
=\frac{\tau(\bar\delta-d)}p.
$$

Combining these estimates yields the coarse bound.
DEPS researcher-0007:P3.1,researcher-0007:P4.1

researcher-0007:P6.1 Cleaned factor component status=conditional
STATEMENT
Assume the hypotheses and notation of researcher-0007:P5.1, and fix $i$. Let $G=(\mathcal X,T',E')$ be a connected bipartite graph with $\mathcal X\subseteq\mathbb F_p^2$, $T'\subseteq\mathcal T_i$, and

$$
E'\subseteq
\{(x,L)\in\mathcal X\times T':x\in L,\ P_L(x)=f(x)\}.
$$

Suppose $G$ has minimum degree at least the integer $k$, and suppose

$$
C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0
\qquad((x,L)\in E').
$$

If

$$
k>\delta_i
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\frac1{\sqrt{p+1}}\le\frac{k}{4p},
$$

then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
Q(x)=f(x)
\qquad(x\in\mathcal X)
$$

and

$$
\frac{|\{x\in\mathbb F_p^2:Q(x)=f(x)\}|}{p^2}
\ge\frac{k}{4p}.
$$
PROOF
For every edge $(x,L)\in E'$, the assigned graph identity and acceptance imply

$$
C_i(x,f(x))=0.
$$

The cleaned-edge hypothesis gives $C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0$, so this is a simple $Z$-root. Choose $b\in\mathcal X$. At least $k>\delta_i$ graph lines of the component pass through $b$. The simple-root Newton recursion from KTZ Lemmas 2.7 and 6.1, applied with weighted degree $\delta_i$, constructs a polynomial $Q$ of total degree at most $d$, agrees with every incident line label, and satisfies

$$
C_i(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0.
$$

The recursion divides only by the certified nonzero scalar $C_{i,Z}(b,f(b))$. Simple-root uniqueness propagates the same $Q$ throughout the connected component, yielding $Q(x)=f(x)$ for every $x\in\mathcal X$.

For the density bound, put

$$
a=\frac{|\mathcal X|}{p^2},
\qquad
b_0=\frac{|T'|}{p(p+1)},
\qquad
\rho=\frac{|E'|}{p^2(p+1)},
$$

and

$$
\kappa=\frac{k}{p},
\qquad
u=\frac1{\sqrt{p+1}}.
$$

Minimum degree and affine incidence mixing give

$$
\rho\ge\frac{\kappa a}{2},
\qquad
\rho\ge\kappa b_0,
\qquad
\rho\le ab_0+\nu\sqrt{ab_0}.
$$

The mixing hypothesis is $\nu\le\kappa/4$. If $a<\kappa/4$, the second and third inequalities imply $b_0<a/9$; substituting this into the upper bound contradicts $\rho\ge\kappa a/2$. If $b_0<\kappa/4$, the first and third inequalities imply $a<b_0$; substitution contradicts $\rho\ge\kappa b_0$. Therefore

$$
a,b_0\ge\frac\kappa4.
$$

Since $Q=f$ on $\mathcal X$, its global agreement is at least $a\ge k/(4p)$.

IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN
Factor $$B=c\prod_{i=1}^s C_i.$$ Then every $C_i$ depends on $Z$ and $$s\le\deg_ZB\le D/d\le K_\eta.$$ The simple-root graph partitions into factor classes. For some $C_i$ there is an accepted subgraph $F$ with $$e(F)\ge c''_\eta\epsilon,
\qquad k(F)>\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),$$ such that $C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ on every line vertex and $C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0$ on every point vertex.
NOTES The cited factorwise source is pending and `audit_exact=false`; the node requires an exact proof in the current chain.
EVIDENCE [{"source_job_id":"researcher-0007","source_response_sha256":"5276991887baf2d63587a698cee2c062d7fe45bc27847def09fdf171dd0106d8","source_step_id":"P1"}]

IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE
Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(C)<p$. If an accepted factor class $F$ has minimum degree greater than $\Delta$, satisfies $C(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ on every line vertex, and has $C_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$ at every point vertex, then there is $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$C\sim Z-Q$$ and $Q(x)=f(x)$ on every point vertex of $F$. Consequently, $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge e(F).$$
NOTES Researcher-0003 P7 is audit-inexact under a rejected compound submission; researcher-0002 P8 confirms component-level simple-root recovery but not this cross-component aggregation.
EVIDENCE [{"source_job_id":"researcher-0003","source_response_sha256":"b14110027e6e43e9df36e71e0b3bcdc789b9a3d28018d7232a7481aa75bf0420","source_step_id":"P7"},{"source_job_id":"researcher-0002","source_response_sha256":"f85203541dd3187a04a81391290a02795cd0b0b67307f53014a0d0d4697e8d3f","source_step_id":"P8"}]

IE-R-CLOSE [reduction/blocked] deps=IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT
The dichotomy in `IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY` and lemmas `IE-L-RANK-INTERP` through `IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT` imply constants $C'_\eta,c'_\eta>0$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C'_\eta(d/p)^{1-\eta}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c'_\eta\epsilon.$$
NOTES The direct branch is immediate; the structured branch uses only $O_\eta(1)$ factors and therefore loses an $\eta$-dependent constant, not a power of $d/p$.
EVIDENCE []

IE-GOAL [theorem/blocked] deps=IE-R-CLOSE
For every fixed $\eta>0$ there exist $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, every $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and every degree-at-most-$d$ affine-line table, $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\eta}$$ implies the existence of one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
NOTES Blocked principally on `IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY` and a fresh exact audit of factor linearization. No roadmap work state is a proof-status claim.
EVIDENCE []
